# Gaza war

This article is about the ongoing war in the Gaza Strip and Israel. For the broader conflicts, see [Gaza–Israel conflict](16346266.md) and [Israeli–Palestinian conflict](46216.md). For previous wars, see [Gaza war (disambiguation)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_war_\(disambiguation\)).

&#x20;

The **Gaza war**[^cite_note-32] is an armed conflict in the [Gaza Strip](12047.md) and [Israel](9282173.md), fought as part of the unresolved [Israeli–Palestinian](46216.md) and [Gaza–Israel](16346266.md) conflicts. The war began on 7 October 2023, when Palestinian militant group [Hamas](13913.md) led [an attack on Israel](74998317.md), in which 1,195 [Israelis](181296.md) and foreign nationals were killed and [251 were taken hostage](75025451.md). Since the start of the Israeli offensive that followed, over 74,000 [Palestinians](23267.md) in Gaza [have been killed](75130785.md);[^cite_note-33][^cite_note-34][^cite_note-35] several studies in *[The Lancet](463899.md)* indicate this figure is likely a significant undercount.[^cite_note-Lancet2025-36][^cite_note-37][^cite_note-38][^cite_note-39] Many scholarly sources and international organizations—including [UN bodies](31769.md), [NGOs](46539.md), and the [International Association of Genocide Scholars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Association_of_Genocide_Scholars)—have concluded that Israel's actions in [Gaza constitute genocide](75668427.md) under international law.[^cite_note-45] Several countries have also [stated that genocide is occurring](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_genocide_recognition) and have supported legal proceedings before the [International Court of Justice](14918.md). Israel and its supporters, including the [United States](3434750.md), [deny](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_genocide_denial) that genocide has occurred.[^cite_note-46][^cite_note-47][^cite_note-48]&#x20;

Following the 7 October attacks, Israel launched a [bombing campaign](76139149.md) and later invaded Gaza on 27 October after clearing militants from its territory. [Israeli forces](39237.md) launched several campaigns, including the [Rafah offensive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafah_offensive), three battles fought around [Khan Yunis](101330.md), and the [siege of North Gaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_of_North_Gaza), culminating in a 2025 [offensive in Gaza City](80801475.md). They have assassinated Hamas leaders inside and outside of Gaza. A [2023 ceasefire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Gaza_war_ceasefire) broke down, and a [second ceasefire in January 2025](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/January_2025_Gaza_war_ceasefire) ended with [a surprise attack by Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/March_2025_Israeli_attacks_on_the_Gaza_Strip) in March. A third ceasefire came into effect on 10 October after Israel and Hamas agreed to phase one of a [US-backed peace plan](81215079.md).[^cite_note-theguardian20251009-49][^cite_note-wsj20251010-50] As part of the peace plan, the [Hamas civil administration in Gaza](38626632.md) resigned on 6 July 2026,[^cite_note-51] and an agreement was reached on 31 July for Hamas and [other militant organisations in Gaza](75192468.md) to disarm, contingent on reconstruction progress and Israel's military withdrawal.[^cite_note-deccanchronicle20260731-52][^cite_note-haaretz20260731-53]&#x20;

The war has resulted in [significant humanitarian costs](75277791.md). A [tightened blockade by Israel](75013719.md) cut off basic necessities, which led to a [partial famine](75828824.md).[^cite_note-54][^cite_note-RIFGS-55][^cite_note-56][^cite_note-57] Around 90% of Gaza's civilian infrastructure has been destroyed and essential services, including water, electricity, and sanitation, have been severely disrupted;[^cite_note-58] large parts are uninhabitable,[^cite_note-amnesty20241205-59] with most hospitals, [religious and cultural landmarks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Destruction_of_cultural_heritage_during_the_Gaza_war),[^cite_note-Ahmed-2024-60] and [educational facilities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attacks_on_schools_during_the_Israeli_invasion_of_Gaza) destroyed.[^cite_note-61][^cite_note-62] Gazan [journalists](75146557.md),[^cite_note-63] [health workers](75657910.md),[^cite_note-Human_Rights_Watch-2024-64] aid workers and other members of civil society have been detained, tortured,[^cite_note-thenewarab20241114-65][^cite_note-TheGuardian2802025-66] and killed.[^cite_note-67] Israel has [detained](75234504.md) thousands of Palestinians from Gaza and the West Bank since the start of the war.[^cite_note-68] Nearly all of the strip's 2.3 million Palestinians have been [forcibly displaced](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forcibly_displaced).[^cite_note-69][^cite_note-70] Over 100,000 Israelis were internally displaced at the height of the conflict.[^cite_note-71] The first day was the deadliest in Israel's history, and the war is the deadliest for Palestinians in the broader conflict.[^cite_note-72]&#x20;

In September 2025, the [UN Commission of Inquiry on the Occupied Palestinian Territory](69971286.md) concluded that four of the five [acts of genocide](143988.md#Definition_of_genocide) as defined by the Genocide Convention had been committed in Gaza since 7 October 2023.[^cite_note-73] [A case](75736183.md) accusing Israel of genocide is being reviewed by the [International Court of Justice](14918.md).[^cite_note-Al_Jazeera-2024-74] Experts and human rights organizations have also stated that Israel and Hamas [have committed other war crimes](75113830.md). Some have described the 7 October attacks as a [genocide or genocidal massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allegations_of_genocide_in_the_October_7_attacks) of Israelis. Torture and [sexual violence](731637.md) have been committed both [by Palestinian militant groups](75436869.md) and [by Israeli forces](76149089.md).[^cite_note-75]&#x20;

Israel has received extensive military and diplomatic [support from the United States](65284610.md).[^cite_note-76] The war has [reverberated regionally](75895273.md), with "[Axis of Resistance](36658251.md)" groups across several Arab countries and [Iran clashing with the US](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attacks_on_US_bases_during_the_Gaza_war) and Israel, including in the [Twelve-Day War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twelve-Day_War) in 2025, and the [2026 Iran war](82533582.md).[^cite_note-nytimes20260228-77] [A year of strikes](75005425.md) between Israel and [Hezbollah](13919.md) led to wars in Lebanon [in 2024](78006796.md) and [in 2026](82549840.md), as well as ongoing [Israeli operations in Syria](78549398.md), which contributed to the [fall of the Assad regime](78548066.md). The war continues to have regional and international repercussions, with [large protests](75044900.md) worldwide as well as a surge of [antisemitism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antisemitism_during_the_Gaza_war) and [anti-Palestinian racism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-Palestinian_racism_during_the_Gaza_war).&#x20;

## Names

Palestinian militant groups refer to the conflict as the "battle of al-Aqsa Flood" ([Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabic_language): معركة طوفان الأقصى), in reference to [Operation al-Aqsa Flood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_al-Aqsa_Flood).[^cite_note-78][^cite_note-79] The [government of Israel](72545811.md) referred to it as the "Iron Swords war" ([Hebrew](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hebrew_language): מִלְחֲמֹת חַרְבוֹת בַּרְזֶל)[^cite_note-80][^cite_note-83] until October 2025, when it began using "War of Redemption".[^cite_note-84][^cite_note-85] Within Israel, it is popularly referred to as the "October 7 war".[^cite_note-86] It has also been referred to as the "Simchat Torah war" because Hamas's attack began on the Jewish holiday of [Simchat Torah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simchat_Torah).[^cite_note-87] Media outlets have variably described it as the "Israel–Hamas war",[^cite_note-88] "war on Gaza",[^cite_note-89] "October 7 war",[^cite_note-90][^cite_note-91] and the "second [Nakba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nakba)".[^cite_note-92][^cite_note-93]&#x20;

## Background

Main article: [Background to the Gaza war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Background_to_the_Gaza_war)

A bar chart from 2008 to before October 2023. 6,407 Palestinians have been killed during this time frame, while a smaller 308 Israelis have been killed.

[Israeli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_casualties_of_war) and [Palestinian deaths](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_casualties_of_war) preceding the 2023 Hamas-led attack on Israel. Most of these deaths were civilians.

[^cite_note-OCHAoPt-94]

[^cite_note-Alfonseca-2023-95]

The Gaza war is part of the [Israeli–Palestinian conflict](46216.md).[^cite_note-96] In the [1948 Palestine war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1948_Palestine_war), [over 700,000 Palestinians fled or were expelled](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1948_Palestinian_expulsion_and_flight) and the [State of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_of_Israel) was [established](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_Declaration_of_Independence) over most of what had been [Mandatory Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mandatory_Palestine), with the exception of two separated territories that became known as the [West Bank](33209.md) and the Gaza Strip.[^cite_note-97] Following the 1967 [Six-Day War](29328.md), Israel occupied both [Palestinian territories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_territories).[^cite_note-El_Deeb-2023-98] The upcoming period witnessed two popular uprisings by [Palestinians](23267.md) against the [Israeli occupation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_occupation): the [First](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Intifada) and [Second Intifadas](71717.md) in 1987 and 2000,[^cite_note-99] with the latter's end seeing [Israel's unilateral withdrawal from Gaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_disengagement_from_Gaza) in 2005.[^cite_note-100][^cite_note-101]&#x20;

Since 2007, the Gaza Strip has been governed by [Hamas](13913.md), an [Islamist](15012.md) militant group, while the West Bank remained under the control of the [Palestinian Authority](24093.md). After Hamas's takeover, Israel imposed a [blockade](19224310.md)[^cite_note-102][^cite_note-Kane-2023-103] that significantly damaged [Gaza's economy](22098325.md).[^cite_note-WBEUUN-104] Israel justified the blockade by citing security concerns,[^cite_note-Meakem-2023-105] but international rights groups have characterized it as [collective punishment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Collective_punishment).[^cite_note-106][^cite_note-107][^cite_note-Abdulrahim-2023-108] By 2023, [UNRWA](32270.md) reported that 81% of people were living below the [poverty level](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poverty_level), with 63% being food insecure and dependent on international assistance.[^cite_note-Alfonseca-2023-95][^cite_note-109]&#x20;

Since 2007, Israel and Hamas, along with other Palestinian militant groups based in Gaza, have engaged in conflict,[^cite_note-Meakem-2023-105][^cite_note-Kane-2023-103][^cite_note-Beauchamp-2023-110] including four wars, in [2008–2009](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_War_\(2008%E2%80%932009\)), [2012](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_Gaza_War), [2014](43243199.md), and [2021](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Israel%E2%80%93Palestine_crisis).[^cite_note-111][^cite_note-112][^cite_note-113] Combined, these conflicts killed approximately [6,400 Palestinians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_casualties_of_war) and [300 Israelis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_casualties_of_war).[^cite_note-114][^cite_note-OCHAoPt-94][^cite_note-Alfonseca-2023-95] In 2018–2019, there was the [Great March of Return](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_March_of_Return), a series of large [organized protests near the Gaza-Israel border](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018%E2%80%932019_Gaza_border_protests) to call for Palestinian refugees' [right to return](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_right_of_return). The [Israel Defense Forces](39237.md) (IDF) violently suppressed the protests, killing hundreds and injuring thousands of Palestinians by sniper fire.[^cite_note-115][^cite_note-116] Soon after a short 2021 conflict, Hamas's military wing, the [Al-Qassam Brigades](1703333.md), started planning an operation against Israel, which became the 7 October attacks.[^cite_note-Klein-2023-117][^cite_note-118] According to diplomats, Hamas had repeatedly said in the months leading up to October 2023 that it did not want another military escalation in Gaza as it would worsen the humanitarian crisis.[^cite_note-Abdulrahim-2023-108]&#x20;

Hamas officials stated that the attack was a response to Israeli crimes against the Palestinian people, citing specifically the [blockade of the Gaza Strip](19224310.md), desecration of the [Al-Aqsa Mosque](65759.md), [Israeli settler violence](47246246.md) against Palestinians, and the [imprisonment of thousands of Palestinians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_prisoners_in_Israel), whom Hamas sought to release by taking Israeli hostages.[^cite_note-aj7oct-invasion-119][^cite_note-McKernan-20231014-120][^cite_note-Pacchiani-2023-121] Numerous commentators have identified the broader context of Israeli occupation as a cause of the war.[^cite_note-Matar-2023-122][^cite_note-123][^cite_note-124] Several human rights organizations, including [Amnesty International](18947898.md),[^cite_note-125] [B'Tselem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B%27Tselem)[^cite_note-126] and [Human Rights Watch](46530.md)[^cite_note-127] have [likened the Israeli occupation to apartheid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_and_apartheid), although supporters of Israel dispute this characterization.[^cite_note-128][^cite_note-129] The [Netanyahu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Netanyahu) government has been criticized within Israel for granting [work permits to Gazan residents](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_permit_regime_in_the_Gaza_Strip), facilitating the transfer of funds to Hamas and pursuing relative calm. These actions have been criticized as having backfired in light of the attacks on 7 October 2023.[^cite_note-Schneider-2023-130][^cite_note-Foreign_Policy-2021-131][^cite_note-The_New_York_Times-2023-132]&#x20;

At the time of the attack, Israel and Saudi Arabia were conducting negotiations to [normalize relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab%E2%80%93Israeli_normalization).[^cite_note-133] Hamas leaders such as [Ismail Haniyeh](41207463.md) cited disrupting this "normalization train" as a motive for the October 7 attacks.[^cite_note-134]&#x20;

## War in Israel and Gaza

For a chronological guide, see [Timeline of the Gaza war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_the_Gaza_war).

For a more comprehensive list, see [List of military engagements during the Gaza war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_military_engagements_during_the_Gaza_war).

### October 7 attacks

Main article: [October 7 attacks](74998317.md)

See also: [List of Palestinian rocket attacks on Israel in 2023](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Palestinian_rocket_attacks_on_Israel_in_2023)











**Clockwise from top**:&#x20;

- Approximate situation on 7–8 October

- Aftermath of a Hamas airstrike on the maternity ward of the [Barzilai Medical Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barzilai_Medical_Center)

- Satellite view of widespread fires in Israeli areas surrounding the Gaza Strip

- Footage of Israeli soldiers securing the area after the [Nova music festival massacre](75007485.md)

On the morning of 7 October 2023,[^cite_note-aj7oct-retaliation-135] during the Jewish holidays of Simchat Torah and [Shemini Atzeret](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shemini_Atzeret) on [Shabbat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shabbat),[^cite_note-136] Hamas announced the start of "[Operation Al-Aqsa Flood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Al-Aqsa_Flood)", firing between 3,000 and 5,000 rockets from the Gaza Strip into Israel within a span of 20 minutes, killing at least five people.[^cite_note-137][^cite_note-aj7oct-escalation-138][^cite_note-McKernan-20231007-139] In the evening, Hamas launched another barrage of 150 rockets.[^cite_note-aj7oct-escalation-138] Simultaneously, around 3,000 Hamas militants[^cite_note-strength1-22] infiltrated Israel from Gaza using trucks, motorcycles, bulldozers, speedboats, and paragliders.[^cite_note-Federman-20231007-140][^cite_note-aj7oct-retaliation-135][^cite_note-Kingsley-2023-141] They took over checkpoints at [Kerem Shalom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kerem_Shalom_border_crossing) and [Erez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erez_Crossing), and created openings in the border fence in five other places.[^cite_note-142]&#x20;

Militants massacred civilians in several [kibbutzim](106200.md),[^cite_note-143][^cite_note-The_Economist-20231012-144] where they took hostages[^cite_note-145] and set fire to homes.[^cite_note-ynet7oct2-146] In [a massacre at an outdoor music festival near Re'im](75007485.md), at least 325 people were killed, with more injured or taken hostage.[^cite_note-Zitun-2023-147][^cite_note-148] In total, 251 people, mostly civilians, were [taken hostage](75025451.md), including[^cite_note-Vinograd-2023-149][^cite_note-TAI_1-150] children, elderly people, and soldiers.[^cite_note-151] Hamas militants also reportedly engaged in mutilation, torture, and [sexual and gender-based violence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sexual_and_gender-based_violence_in_the_2023_Hamas-led_attack_on_Israel).[^cite_note-washingtonpost20231125-152][^cite_note-153][^cite_note-NYT28Dec2023-154]&#x20;

The attack was not entirely unexpected; but "Israeli officials received Hamas' battle plans" but deemed them aspirational and dismissed specific warnings.[^cite_note-155] The 7 October attacks were described as "an intelligence failure for the ages"[^cite_note-156] and a "[failure of imagination](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Failure_of_imagination)" on the part of the Israeli government.[^cite_note-157] It later emerged that abnormal Hamas movements had been detected the previous day by Israeli intelligence, but the military's alert level was not raised and political leaders were not informed.[^cite_note-158]&#x20;

*[The Economist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Economist)* noted that "the assault dwarf\[ed] all other mass murders of Israeli civilians", and that "the last time before October 7th that this many Jews were murdered on a single day was during [the Holocaust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Holocaust)."[^cite_note-The_Economist-20231012-144] According to both Hamas officials and external observers, the attack was a calculated effort to create a "permanent" state of war and revive the Palestinian cause.[^cite_note-gambit-159][^cite_note-160]&#x20;

### Initial Israeli counter-operation (October 2023)

For a chronological guide, see [Timeline of the Gaza war (7 October 2023 – 27 October 2023)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_the_Gaza_war_\(7_October_2023_%E2%80%93_27_October_2023\)).

Main articles: [2023 Israeli blockade of the Gaza Strip](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Israeli_blockade_of_the_Gaza_Strip), [Israeli bombing of the Gaza Strip](76139149.md), and [Evacuation of the northern Gaza Strip](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evacuation_of_the_northern_Gaza_Strip)











**Clockwise from top**:&#x20;

- Palestinian infant receiving treatment following an Israeli airstrike in Deir el-Balah, Gaza Strip

- A Palestinian refugee carries his injured grandchildren during the Israeli bombing of the [Nuseirat refugee camp](18168164.md)

- Building in the Gaza Strip being destroyed by Israeli missiles

- Damage following an Israeli airstrike on the El-Remal area in Gaza City on October 2023

- Injured Palestinians including children receive treatment at Al-Aqsa Hospital following an Israeli airstrike on the Nuseirat refugee camp

The IDF began Israel's counter-attack several hours after the Hamas-led invasion.[^cite_note-161] The first helicopters sent to support the military reached the Israeli areas surrounding the Gaza Strip an hour after the fighting began.[^cite_note-Zitun-2023-147] Their crews encountered difficulties in determining which places were occupied by invading militants, and distinguishing between Israeli civilians, IDF soldiers, and Palestinian militants on the ground.[^cite_note-Zitun-2023-147] A June 2024 UN report[^cite_note-162][^cite_note-163] and a July 2024 *Haaretz* investigation revealed that the IDF ordered the [Hannibal Directive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hannibal_Directive) to be used, killing an unknown number of Israeli civilians and soldiers.[^cite_note-Yaniv-2024-164][^cite_note-AJ-2024-07-09-165][^cite_note-ABC_News-2024-166]&#x20;

In a televised broadcast, [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md), [Prime Minister of Israel](24898.md), announced that the country was at war.[^cite_note-Kingsley-2023-141] He threatened to "turn all the places where Hamas is organized and hiding into cities of ruins", called Gaza "the city of evil", and urged its residents to leave.[^cite_note-167][^cite_note-aj7oct-invasion-119] Overnight, Israel's [Security Cabinet](22256400.md) voted to act to bring about the "destruction of the military and governmental capabilities of [Hamas](13913.md) and [Palestinian Islamic Jihad](13093253.md)".[^cite_note-168] The [Israel Electric Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_Electric_Corporation), which supplies 80% of Gaza's electricity, cut off power to the area.[^cite_note-Gritten-2023-169] The IDF declared a "state of readiness for war",[^cite_note-aj7oct-escalation-138] mobilized tens of thousands of army [reservists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reservist),[^cite_note-aj7oct-retaliation-135][^cite_note-Gritten-2023-169] and declared a state of emergency for areas within 80 kilometers (50 mi) of Gaza.[^cite_note-Bayer-2023-170] While [Ben Gurion Airport](113149.md) and [Ramon Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramon_Airport) remained operational, multiple airlines cancelled flights.[^cite_note-171] On 9 or 10 October, Hamas offered to release all civilian hostages held in Gaza if Israel would call off its planned invasion of the Gaza Strip, but the Israeli government rejected the offer.[^cite_note-172]&#x20;









**Clockwise from top**:&#x20;
