- A newborn baby killed as a result of an Israeli airstrike

- People fleeing following an Israeli airstrike on a house in Deir el-Balah, Gaza Strip

- Video interviews conducted with several survivors of Israeli airstrikes in October 2023

Following the surprise attack, the [Israeli Air Force](177619.md) conducted airstrikes that they said targeted Hamas targets,[^cite_note-aj7oct-escalation-138][^cite_note-Bayer-2023-170][^cite_note-Knell-2023-173] employing its [artificial intelligence](1164.md) *Habsora* ("The Gospel") software.[^cite_note-174][^cite_note-Abraham-2023-175] These airstrikes killed, on average, 350 persons per day during the first 20 days.[^cite_note-176] Israel rescued two hostages before declaring a [state of war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_of_war) for the first time since the [1973 Yom Kippur War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1973_Yom_Kippur_War).[^cite_note-177][^cite_note-178] On 9 October, Defense Minister Gallant announced a "complete siege" of the Gaza Strip, cutting off electricity and blocking the entry of food and fuel.[^cite_note-179] Gallant backed down under pressure from US President [Joe Biden](145422.md), and a deal was struck ten days later to allow aid into Gaza.[^cite_note-Fergal_Keane-2024-180] The first such aid convoy entered Gaza on 21 October,[^cite_note-181] while fuel did not arrive until November.[^cite_note-182]&#x20;

On 13 October, the [IDF](39237.md) ordered all civilians in [Gaza City](55387.md) to evacuate to areas south of the [Wadi Gaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wadi_Gaza)[^cite_note-183] within 24 hours. The Hamas Authority for Refugee Affairs told residents in northern Gaza to defy those orders.[^cite_note-184] The Israeli order was widely condemned as "outrageous" and "impossible", and calls were made for it to be reversed.[^cite_note-185] As a part of the order, the IDF outlined a six-hour window on 13 October for refugees to flee south along specified routes.[^cite_note-186] [An explosion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attacks_on_Palestinians_evacuating_Gaza_City#13_October_2023) along one of the safe routes killed 70 Palestinians. Israel and Hamas blamed each other for the attack.[^cite_note-187] The IDF said Hamas set up roadblocks to keep Gaza residents from evacuating.[^cite_note-188] Israeli officials, foreign governments and intergovernmental organizations condemned Hamas's alleged use of [hospitals and civilians as human shields](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Use_of_human_shields_by_Hamas), which Hamas contested.[^cite_note-189][^cite_note-190]&#x20;

On 17 October, Israel bombed areas of southern Gaza.[^cite_note-191] Late in the evening, an [explosion occurred](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Ahli_Arab_Hospital_explosion) in the parking lot of the [Al-Ahli Arabi Baptist Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Ahli_Arabi_Baptist_Hospital) in the center of Gaza City, killing hundreds. The ongoing conflict prevented independent on-site analysis.[^cite_note-192] Palestinian statements that it was an Israeli airstrike were denied by the IDF, which stated that the explosion resulted from a failed rocket launch by [Palestinian Islamic Jihad](13093253.md),[^cite_note-193] who denied any involvement.[^cite_note-194]&#x20;

### Initial invasion and first ceasefire (October–November 2023)

For a chronological guide, see [Timeline of the Gaza war (28 October – 23 November 2023)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_the_Gaza_war_\(28_October_%E2%80%93_23_November_2023\)) and [Timeline of the Gaza war (24 November 2023 – 11 January 2024)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_the_Gaza_war_\(24_November_2023_%E2%80%93_11_January_2024\)).

Further information: [2023 Gaza war ceasefire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Gaza_war_ceasefire)







Israeli military during ground operations in Gaza

On 27 October, after building up an invasion force of over 100,000 soldiers, the IDF launched a large-scale ground incursion into parts of northern Gaza.[^cite_note-195][^cite_note-196] Israeli airstrikes targeted the area around [al-Quds hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Quds_hospital),[^cite_note-Jackson-2023-197] where around 14,000 civilians were believed to be sheltering.[^cite_note-Jackson-2023-197] The following day, the IDF [struck Jabalia refugee camp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/31_October_2023_Jabalia_refugee_camp_airstrike), killing 50 and wounding 150 Palestinians. Israel said the attack killed a senior Hamas commander, whose presence Hamas denied, and dozens of militants.[^cite_note-198][^cite_note-199][^cite_note-200] The attack resulted in several ambassador recalls.[^cite_note-201][^cite_note-Ramos-Bolivia-202][^cite_note-203]&#x20;

| External videos |
| --- |
| video icon [Gazan child speaks of having to carry a decapitated body after Israeli strike on Jabalia](https://www.irishtimes.com/video/world/middle-east/2023/11/04/gazan-child-speaks-of-having-to-carry-decapitated-body-after-israeli-strike-on-jabalia/) (via *[The Irish Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Irish_Times)*) |

On 31 October, Israel [bombed a six-story apartment building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Engineer%27s_Building_airstrike) in central Gaza, killing at least 106 civilians including 54 children in what Human Rights Watch called an "apparent war crime".[^cite_note-204] On 1 November, the first group of evacuees left Gaza for Egypt. Five hundred evacuees, comprising critically wounded and foreign nationals, were evacuated over several days.[^cite_note-205] On 22 November, Israel and Hamas reached [a temporary ceasefire agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Gaza_war_ceasefire), providing for a four-day pause[^cite_note-Al_Jazeera-20231122-206] in hostilities, the release of 50 hostages held in Gaza,[^cite_note-Al_Jazeera-20231122-206][^cite_note-Burke-2023-207] and the release of approximately 150 Palestinian women and children incarcerated by Israel.[^cite_note-Burke-2023-207]&#x20;



13-year old Israeli hostage released by Hamas during the [first ceasefire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Gaza_war_ceasefire), 26 November 2023

Following the introduction of a Qatari-brokered [truce](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Gaza_war_ceasefire) on 24 November 2023, active fighting in Gaza ceased. Hamas [exchanged some hostages for Palestinian prisoners](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Israeli%E2%80%93Palestinian_prisoner_exchange) held by Israel.[^cite_note-208] Israel arrested almost as many Palestinians as it released during the truce.[^cite_note-209] Prisoner exchanges continued until 28 November, when both Israel and Hamas accused each other of violating the truce.[^cite_note-210][^cite_note-211] On 30 November, in a "last-minute agreement", Hamas released eight hostages in exchange for the release of 30 imprisoned Palestinians and a one-day truce extension.[^cite_note-212] The truce expired on 1 December, as Israel and Hamas blamed each other for failing to agree on an extension.[^cite_note-213]&#x20;

#### Strategy



Israeli soldiers in a [Namer APC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Namer_APC) before invasion of the Gaza Strip

When Israeli troops entered the Gaza Strip and began to maintain a persistent physical presence in the region beginning on 27 October 2023, Israel's military adopted a communications strategy designed to preserve operational flexibility and security.[^cite_note-Invasion-214] The United States urged Israel to avoid a full-scale invasion and to instead conduct "surgical" operations to avoid casualties and a regional escalation.[^cite_note-215] When the IDF initiated [Operation Gideon's Chariots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Gideon%27s_Chariots) in May 2025, the systematic destruction of infrastructure became an explicit component of official military policy and was implemented with increased intensity. Notably, private contractors were engaged in the operation.[^cite_note-216]&#x20;

The military strategies employed by Hamas have historically encompassed a blend of conventional and insurgent tactics.[^cite_note-217] As a result of the war, the group has shifted "back into a guerrilla fighting force", employing the use of [hit-and-run operations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hit-and-run_tactics) and operating in smaller cells of fighters, aiming to demonstrate that they are capable of continuing to fight a prolonged war.[^cite_note-218] Hamas's strategy also relies on the [underground tunnel system](43413500.md), which can be used as shelter for Hamas militants and leaders, storing weapons, and detaining hostages.[^cite_note-underground-219]&#x20;

Sources close to Hamas leadership said that they counted on the international pressure for Israel to end the siege due to mounting civilian casualties and achieve a ceasefire.[^cite_note-quagmire-220][^cite_note-underground-219] Hamas believed that together with strikes by Hezbollah and Iran and the uprising in the West Bank, they would cause Israel to collapse, while Israeli retaliation against Gaza would kill many civilians but undermine their crucial international support.[^cite_note-theatlantic20231101-221] Some, including Israel, have accused Hamas of deliberately using Palestinian civilians as human shields.[^cite_note-theatlantic20231101-221][^cite_note-quagmire-220] Such claims have been disputed. In February 2024, *[Forensic Architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forensic_Architecture)* (FA) examined the evidence that Israel provided at the ICJ regarding Hamas's alleged use of civilian facilities for military use. FA said that much of the Israeli evidence was misleading or false.[^cite_note-222][^cite_note-223]&#x20;

### Resumption of hostilities (December 2023 – January 2024)

For a chronological guide, see [Timeline of the Gaza war (12 January – 6 May 2024)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_the_Gaza_war_\(12_January_%E2%80%93_6_May_2024\)).

Israel adopted a grid system to order precise evacuations within Gaza. It was criticized as confusing and inaccessible, due to the lack of electricity and internet connectivity in Gaza. Some evacuation instructions were vague or contradictory,[^cite_note-224][^cite_note-225] and Israel sometimes struck areas it had told people to evacuate to.[^cite_note-226][^cite_note-227][^cite_note-228] Law experts called these warnings ineffective.[^cite_note-229] Amnesty International found no evidence of Hamas targets at the sites of some strikes, and requested that they be investigated as possible war crimes.[^cite_note-230] On 6 December, [Refaat Alareer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Refaat_Alareer), a prominent writer in Gaza, was killed by an Israeli airstrike,[^cite_note-231] after which his poem "[If I Must Die](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/If_I_Must_Die)" was widely circulated.[^cite_note-232]&#x20;



An aerial photo of displaced Palestinians waiting in northern Nuseirat to return to their homes in Gaza

By December, IDF troops had reached the centers of [Khan Yunis](101330.md), [Jabalia](453723.md), and [Shuja'iyya](20956585.md).[^cite_note-233] Intensified bombing pushed Palestinian civilians south to Rafah.[^cite_note-234] Between 7 and 10 December, Israel [detained more than 150 men](75234504.md#December_7_mass_detention); according to Israel, they surrendered *en masse*,[^cite_note-235][^cite_note-236] but this account was disputed by several publications.[^cite_note-237][^cite_note-238][^cite_note-239] On 15 December, the IDF [killed three Israeli hostages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Killing_of_Alon_Shamriz,_Yotam_Haim,_and_Samer_Talalka) in a friendly fire incident.[^cite_note-Tabachnick-2023-240][^cite_note-241][^cite_note-242]&#x20;

On 1 January 2024, Israel withdrew from neighborhoods in North Gaza.[^cite_note-243] On 15 January, Israeli Defense Minister [Yoav Gallant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yoav_Gallant) said the most intense fighting in the north of the Gaza Strip had ended, and a new phase of low-intensity fighting was about to begin.[^cite_note-244] By 18 January, the IDF, who had previously stated that Hamas control over North Gaza was "dismantled", reported that Hamas had significantly rebuilt its fighting strength in North Gaza.[^cite_note-245]&#x20;

On 22 January, 24 IDF soldiers died in the deadliest day for the IDF since the invasion began. Of these, 21 died when Palestinian militants fired an RPG at a tank, causing adjacent buildings to collapse.[^cite_note-246][^cite_note-247][^cite_note-248] On 29 January, Israeli forces [killed Hind Rajab](76056228.md), a five-year-old girl, and six of her family members when the car they were driving was struck by Israeli tank and machine gun fire; two rescue workers attempting to retrieve Rajab were also killed.[^cite_note-249] The [Red Crescent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_Crescent) released the audio from Rajab's phone call with rescue workers, causing international outrage over her death.[^cite_note-250]&#x20;

### Build-up to the Rafah offensive (February–April 2024)

Main article: [Background of the Rafah offensive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Background_of_the_Rafah_offensive)



An aerial view showing destruction in Rafah after Israeli forces withdrawal and as the ceasefire took hold, Gaza Strip



An aerial view of the [Flour Massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flour_Massacre) captured by an Israeli drone, February 2024

Between February and May 2024, preparations to invade Rafah became a dominant theme in Israeli officials' public rhetoric. On 12 February, Israel conducted a [hostage rescue operation in Rafah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Rafah_hostage_raid) along with a [bombing campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/12_February_2024_Rafah_strikes).[^cite_note-251] Food supplies increasingly became an issue. On 5 February, Israeli shelling damaged a marked UNRWA convoy, forcing UNRWA to suspend its operations for almost three weeks, affecting 200,000 people.[^cite_note-252] On 29 February, Israeli forces [opened fire on Palestinians that swarmed aid trucks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flour_Massacre) in southwest of Gaza City, killing 100 and wounding 750. Some of the victims were run over by trucks as panic spread.[^cite_note-253] Survivors described it as an intentional [ambush](1905.md).[^cite_note-254][^cite_note-255] On 1 March, the United States announced it would begin to airdrop food aid into Gaza.[^cite_note-256] Some experts called the initiative performative, saying it would not alleviate the food situation.[^cite_note-257] During his [State of the Union Address](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_State_of_the_Union_Address), Biden announced that a temporary port on Gaza's coast would be constructed for aid delivery.[^cite_note-258]&#x20;

[Al-Shifa Hospital](21218880.md), previously [besieged in November 2023](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Shifa_Hospital_siege), was raided again between 18 March and 1 April.[^cite_note-259] Israeli forces killed Faiq al-Mabhouh, who they said was head of the operations directorate of Hamas's internal security service. Hamas said al-Mabhouh was in charge of civil law enforcement and had been coordinating aid deliveries to north Gaza.[^cite_note-260][^cite_note-261] The IDF said it killed 200 militants in the hospital fighting, including senior Hamas leaders, while also arresting 500 confirmed militants; this account was disputed.[^cite_note-262][^cite_note-263] Survivors denied that militants had organized on the hospital grounds.[^cite_note-264] Israeli forces were accused of leaving the hospital with blown out walls and blackened frame, and of killing 400 Palestinians.[^cite_note-265][^cite_note-266][^cite_note-267]&#x20;

A March UN Security Council resolution calling for an immediate ceasefire in Gaza for [Ramadan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramadan)[^cite_note-aljazeera20240325-268] was ignored by the IDF.[^cite_note-269] On 1 April, seven international aid workers from [World Central Kitchen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Central_Kitchen) (WCK) were killed in an [Israeli airstrike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Central_Kitchen_aid_convoy_attack) south of [Deir al-Balah](1470463.md).[^cite_note-270][^cite_note-Magdy-2024-271][^cite_note-272] WCK, who said their vehicles were clearly marked and their location known to Israel, subsequently withdrew from Gaza alongside [ANERA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ANERA) and [Project HOPE](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Project_HOPE).[^cite_note-Magdy-2024-271][^cite_note-273] On 4 April, Israel opened the [Erez Crossing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erez_Crossing) for the first time since 7 October after US pressure.[^cite_note-274]&#x20;

By 6 March, Israel had completed a new east–west road in Gaza. It was intended to mobilize troops and supplies, to connect and defend IDF positions on [al-Rashid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Rashid_Street_\(Gaza\)) and [Salah al-Din streets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salah_al-Din_street), and prevent people in the south of Gaza from returning to the north.[^cite_note-275] On 7 April, Israel withdrew from the south Gaza Strip, with only one brigade remaining in the [Netzarim Corridor](76319177.md) in the north.[^cite_note-276] Displaced Palestinians began to return.[^cite_note-277] Israel planned to initiate its [ground offensive in Rafah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafah_offensive) around mid-April, but postponed to consider its response to the [Iranian strikes on Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/April_2024_Iranian_strikes_against_Israel).[^cite_note-278] On 25 April, Israel intensified strikes on Rafah ahead of its threatened invasion.[^cite_note-279][^cite_note-280]&#x20;

### Beginning of the Rafah offensive (May–July 2024)

For a chronological guide, see [Timeline of the Gaza war (7 May – 12 July 2024)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_the_Gaza_war_\(7_May_%E2%80%93_12_July_2024\)).

Main article: [Rafah offensive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafah_offensive)



An aerial view of [Al-Mawasi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Mawasi) area where displaced Palestinians live in tents, Gaza Strip

On 6 May, the IDF ordered 100,000 civilians in eastern Rafah to evacuate to [Al-Mawasi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Mawasi), west of Khan Yunis.[^cite_note-281] Later that day, Hamas announced that it had accepted the terms of a ceasefire brokered by Egypt and Qatar.[^cite_note-Ni-2024-282] The deal included a 6-week ceasefire and exchange of prisoners.[^cite_note-283] However, Israel rejected this deal,[^cite_note-rejected-284] saying it would continue to negotiate while the military operation on Rafah was ongoing to "exert military pressure on Hamas".[^cite_note-Burke-2024-285][^cite_note-Gritten-2024-286] On 31 May, the United States announced a [ceasefire framework](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Hamas_war_ceasefire_framework).[^cite_note-287]&#x20;

The same day, the IDF entered the outskirts of Rafah,[^cite_note-Guardian20240506-288][^cite_note-289][^cite_note-290] seizing control of the Gaza side of the [Rafah Crossing](10475618.md) to Egypt the following day.[^cite_note-291][^cite_note-292] By 15 May, an estimated 600,000 had fled Rafah and another 100,000 from the north, according to the United Nations.[^cite_note-293]&#x20;



Israeli [Merkava](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Merkava) tanks at the [Rafah Border Crossing](10475618.md)

On 24 May, the [United Nations](31769.md) said only 906 aid truckloads had reached Gaza since Israel's Rafah operation began.[^cite_note-294] Israel [bombed the Tel al-Sultan displacement camp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tel_al-Sultan_attack) in Rafah on 26 May, killing at least 45 people, allegedly including two senior Hamas officials.[^cite_note-Amnesty_International-2024-295][^cite_note-296][^cite_note-297] This provoked a skirmish between Egyptian and Israeli soldiers at the Gaza border in which one Egyptian soldier was killed.[^cite_note-298] Less than 48 hours afterwards, another evacuation zone, the [Al-Mawasi refugee camp, was bombed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/May_2024_Al-Mawasi_refugee_camp_attack), killing at least 21.[^cite_note-Amnesty_International-2024-295][^cite_note-299][^cite_note-300] The IDF denied involvement.[^cite_note-301]&#x20;

On 6 June, Israel [bombed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Sardi_school_attack) a school in the [Nuseirat refugee camp](18168164.md), killing dozens. Two days later, Israel [attacked Nuseirat refugee camp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuseirat_refugee_camp_massacre) which [resulted in the rescue of four hostages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Nuseirat_rescue_operation)[^cite_note-302][^cite_note-303] and the deaths of 274 Palestinians.[^cite_note-304] On 27 June, Israeli forces [re-invaded the al-Shuja'iyya neighborhood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Battle_of_Shuja%27iyya_\(2024\)).[^cite_note-305] According to [Middle East Monitor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle_East_Monitor) and [ReliefWeb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ReliefWeb), between 4 July and 10 August, Israel attacked 21 schools in Gaza, killing 274 people.[^cite_note-306][^cite_note-307]&#x20;

### Rafah, Khan Yunis, and general bombardment (July–September 2024)

For a chronological guide, see [Timeline of the Gaza war (13 July – 26 September 2024)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_the_Gaza_war_\(13_July_%E2%80%93_26_September_2024\)).

UN analysis covering killed Palestinian civilians between November 2023 and April 2024.

[^cite_note-308]

[^cite_note-axios20241108-309]

1. Children (44.0%)

2. Women (26.0%)

3. Men (30.0%)

On 22 July, the IDF began a [second invasion of Khan Yunis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Battle_of_Khan_Yunis).[^cite_note-310][^cite_note-Al-Mughrabi-2024-311] Israel ordered the evacuation of the eastern part of Khan Yunis;[^cite_note-312] the Gaza health ministry said that [73 people were killed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/July_2024_Khan_Yunis_attack) during the first day of the attack.[^cite_note-Al-Mughrabi-2024-311] Footage from an Israeli drone surfaced showing the destruction of the Grand Mosque in Khan Yunis.[^cite_note-313] A [third, month-long battle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Third_Battle_of_Khan_Yunis) ended on 30 August when the IDF withdrew its 98th battalion from Khan Yunis and Deir el-Balah, stating it had killed over 250 Palestinian militants.[^cite_note-314]&#x20;

On 13 July, at least 90 were killed and 300 injured in [an Israeli strike](77347017.md) on [Al-Mawasi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Mawasi), and 22 were killed in [an Israeli strike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/July_2024_al-Shati_refugee_camp_attack) targeting people gathered to pray in the [Al-Shati refugee camp](16296150.md).[^cite_note-315][^cite_note-316][^cite_note-317] On 10 August, at least 80 Palestinians were killed in [Israeli airstrikes on Al-Tabaeen school](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Tabaeen_school_attack).[^cite_note-318] The IDF said it had killed 200 militants and discovered dozens of weapons in Tel al-Sultan.[^cite_note-319] On 10 September, [Israeli missile strikes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/September_2024_al-Mawasi_strike) on a tent encampment in Al-Mawasi killed 19 to 40 people.[^cite_note-320][^cite_note-321][^cite_note-322]&#x20;

### Continued operations throughout Gaza (October–December 2024)

For a chronological guide, see [Timeline of the Gaza war (27 September 2024 – 16 October 2024)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_the_Gaza_war_\(27_September_2024_%E2%80%93_16_October_2024\)), [Timeline of the Gaza war (17 October – 26 November 2024)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_the_Gaza_war_\(17_October_%E2%80%93_26_November_2024\)), and [Timeline of the Gaza war (27 November 2024 – 18 January 2025)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_the_Gaza_war_\(27_November_2024_%E2%80%93_18_January_2025\)).

In October, [Israeli airstrikes on Shuhada al-Aqsa mosque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/October_2024_Deir_al-Balah_mosque_bombing) in [Deir el-Balah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deir_el-Balah) and a school in central Gaza killed at least 26 Palestinians and injured over 93.[^cite_note-323][^cite_note-324][^cite_note-325] An [Israeli strike on Rufaida school](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/October_2024_Rufaida_school_attack), which was serving as a shelter for displaced people in Deir el-Balah, killed at least 28 people and injured 54.[^cite_note-326][^cite_note-327][^cite_note-328] On 8 October, the IDF began to [encircle Jabalia camp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_battle_of_Jabalia), killing several Palestinian militants and civilians in air strikes and street battles.[^cite_note-Kouddous-2024-329][^cite_note-330] The IDF's operations in Jabalia continued for the rest of October.[^cite_note-331] During that month and November, strikes on Jabalia killed hundreds of people.[^cite_note-332]&#x20;

The IDF was accused of [blocking aid delivery to the Gaza Strip](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_blockade_of_aid_delivery_to_the_Gaza_Strip) by allowing looting gangs to target aid convoys.[^cite_note-washingtonpost20241118-333] On 16 November 98 out of 109 food trucks carrying UN aid from Kerem Shalom crossing were [looted in Israeli-controlled areas of Gaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kerem_Shalom_aid_convoy_looting).[^cite_note-washingtonpost20241118-333][^cite_note-334][^cite_note-335] The Abu Shabab clan, a rival of Hamas, was widely blamed for the attacks.[^cite_note-haaretz20250605-336] On 1 December, the UN suspended its aid shipments through the crossing, blaming Israel for failing to "ensure safe conditions for delivering relief supplies."[^cite_note-337]&#x20;

On 16 October, IDF ground forces [killed Yahya Sinwar](78140031.md) in a shootout in [Tal as-Sultan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tel_al-Sultan_refugee_camp).[^cite_note-338] Biden urged Israel to end the war after Sinwar's death.[^cite_note-339]&#x20;

#### Siege of northern Gaza

See also: [Israeli generals' plan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_generals%27_plan) and [Siege of North Gaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_of_North_Gaza)



A street in northern Gaza pictured in the aftermath of the siege during the initial phase of the 2025 ceasefire

On 13 October, senior IDF officials told *Haaretz* that the government was not seeking to revive hostage talks and that political leadership was pushing for the annexation of parts of the Gaza Strip.[^cite_note-340] In later October, Israel's [siege of North Gaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_of_North_Gaza) intensified and daily aid shipments dropped significantly. Eyewitnesses reported the shelling of hospitals, razing of shelters, and abductions of men and boys, leading to speculation that Israel had decided to implement the ["generals' plan"](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_generals%27_plan): turning the northern Strip into a closed military zone and declaring all who remained to be combatants.[^cite_note-341]&#x20;

The IDF continued its encirclement of Jabalia by sending tanks to [Beit Lahia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beit_Lahia) and [Beit Hanoun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beit_Hanoun) and issuing evacuation orders.[^cite_note-342] On 24 October, an IDF attack destroyed at least 10 residential buildings in the Jabalia refugee camp. According to an assessment by Gaza Civil Defense, 150 people were killed or injured.[^cite_note-343] Food aid to Gaza reached a new low in October at an average of 30 trucks per day, or less than 6% of the daily pre-war average.[^cite_note-344] The UN warned that the situation had become "apocalyptic" and that "the entire Palestinian population in North Gaza is at imminent risk of dying from disease, famine and violence".[^cite_note-345]&#x20;

On 24 November, Israel issued new evacuation orders, triggering another round of displacements in Jabalia.[^cite_note-346] UNRWA said in November that Israel had rejected nine attempts and obstructed an additional 82 to deliver aid to the 60,000 to 70,000 civilians remaining in north Gaza.[^cite_note-347] On 5 December, [Israeli Army Radio](5242192.md) announced that 18,000 Palestinians had been evacuated from Beit Lahia.[^cite_note-348]&#x20;

### Second ceasefire (January–March 2025)

For a chronological guide, see [Timeline of the Gaza war (19 January 2025 – 17 March 2025)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_the_Gaza_war_\(19_January_2025_%E2%80%93_17_March_2025\)).

Main article: [January 2025 Gaza war ceasefire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/January_2025_Gaza_war_ceasefire)



[Liri Albag](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liri_Albag) in the first meeting with her family after being released from Hamas captivity on 25 January 2025.



Yarden Bibas, whose [wife and children](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kidnapping_of_the_Bibas_family) were later returned dead, meets his father and sister for the first time after his release from Hamas captivity on 1 February 2025.

On 15 January 2025, an agreement was announced, through Qatari mediation, in which Hamas agreed to release a number of Israeli hostages held in the Gaza Strip in exchange for Hamas militants and other Palestinians held in Israeli prisons. The two parties also agreed to a ceasefire,[^cite_note-349] which went into effect on the morning of 19 January 2025.[^cite_note-350] On 27 January, tens of thousands of Palestinians began a mass return to northern Gaza after Israel opened a corridor for civilian movement.[^cite_note-351] Hamas said that Israel had violated the terms of the ceasefire, and announced the suspension of the release of Israeli hostages on 10 February.[^cite_note-352] After Netanyahu and US President [Donald Trump](4848272.md) threatened to restart fighting in Gaza,[^cite_note-353] Hamas relented on 13 February,[^cite_note-354] allowing the release of hostages to begin again two days later.[^cite_note-355]&#x20;



Return of displaced people via Al-Rasheed Street after ceasefire, January 2025, Gaza Strip

Negotiations for implementing the second phase of the ceasefire, intended to see the release of all remaining living hostages, the withdrawal of the Israeli military from Gaza and a permanent end to the war, were supposed to begin in February, but never happened.[^cite_note-356][^cite_note-357] Israel endorsed a US plan to extend the Gaza truce for the [Ramadan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramadan) and [Passover](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Passover) periods, but Hamas refused and demanded the second phase be implemented;[^cite_note-358][^cite_note-359] Netanyahu ceased the entry of aid to Gaza the next day.[^cite_note-360][^cite_note-361] This was condemned by Egypt as a violation of the ceasefire, which stipulated that phase one would automatically be extended as long as phase two negotiations were in progress.[^cite_note-362][^cite_note-363] On 9 March, Israeli Energy Minister [Eli Cohen](46195208.md) ordered a halt to supply of Israeli electricity to Gaza.[^cite_note-364]&#x20;

In January, Hamas said it had recruited thousands of new fighters during the war. Then U.S. Secretary of State Antony Blinken estimated that "Hamas has recruited almost as many new militants as it has lost."[^cite_note-365] *Reuters* reported the number of new recruits, based on U.S. intelligence, was 10,000 to 15,000.[^cite_note-366]&#x20;

### Israeli attacks resume (March–April 2025)

For a chronological guide, see [Timeline of the Gaza war (18 March 2025 – 15 May 2025)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_the_Gaza_war_\(18_March_2025_%E2%80%93_15_May_2025\)).

Main article: [March 2025 Israeli attacks on the Gaza Strip](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/March_2025_Israeli_attacks_on_the_Gaza_Strip)



Displaced Palestinians receive food from charitable Tekiya during Ramadan in Deir el-Balah, Gaza Strip.

On 18 March, Israel ended the ceasefire by launching a [surprise attack](2601678.md), killing over 400 people.[^cite_note-367][^cite_note-368][^cite_note-369] Both sides blamed the other; internationally, the strikes stymied hopes for a lasting ceasefire.[^cite_note-370][^cite_note-371] Israel chose to launch the attack on the day Netanyahu would testify in his [corruption trial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trial_of_Benjamin_Netanyahu), forcing the legal proceedings to be postponed.[^cite_note-372] Multiple senior members of Gaza's government and the Hamas political bureau were killed during this round of fighting, including [Issam al-Da'alis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Issam_al-Da%27alis) and [Salah al-Bardawil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salah_al-Bardawil).[^cite_note-373][^cite_note-374] [Palestinian Islamic Jihad](13093253.md) spokesman [Abu Hamza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abu_Hamza_\(spokesperson\)) was also killed.[^cite_note-375][^cite_note-376] On 25 March, hundreds[^cite_note-377] to thousands[^cite_note-378] of Gazan Palestinians [protested against Hamas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Gaza_Strip_anti-Hamas_protests) and the war. The protests were caused by [war-weariness](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War-weariness) and dissatisfaction with Hamas's abuse of civilians, and suppression of [freedom of speech](21401843.md) and [of the press](105323.md).[^cite_note-379]&#x20;

On 19 March, the IDF said that it had created a "partial buffer" and partially recaptured the center of the [Netzarim Corridor](76319177.md).[^cite_note-380][^cite_note-381] On 23 March, IDF troops [fired on humanitarian vehicles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafah_paramedic_massacre), in southern Rafah, killing 15 medics; their bodies were found one week later.[^cite_note-382][^cite_note-383] On 9 April, Israeli warplanes [bombed a residential building in Shuja'iyya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/April_2025_Shuja%27iyya_airstrike), killing over 35 Palestinians and wounding at least 70.[^cite_note-384][^cite_note-385] On 12 April, the IDF announced that it had encircled Rafah, and planned to seize portions of it while ordering large-scale evacuations of its population.[^cite_note-386]&#x20;

### Renewed Israeli offensive (May–September 2025)

For a chronological guide, see [Timeline of the Gaza war (16 May 2025 – 19 August 2025)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_the_Gaza_war_\(16_May_2025_%E2%80%93_19_August_2025\)) and [Timeline of the Gaza war (20 August 2025 – 2 October 2025)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_the_Gaza_war_\(20_August_2025_%E2%80%93_2_October_2025\)).

In early May, Israel announced plans to expand the Gaza offensive and mobilized thousands of reservists.[^cite_note-387][^cite_note-388] On 8 May, two Israeli airstrikes on the last restaurant in Gaza City and a simultaneous strike on a crowded nearby market killed at least 33 people.[^cite_note-389][^cite_note-390] On 13 May, [Israeli airstrikes struck the compound](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Gaza_European_Hospital_strikes) of the [Gaza European Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_European_Hospital) in Khan Yunis, killing [Mohammed Sinwar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammed_Sinwar) and [Muhammad Shabana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhammad_Shabana) who were in underground tunnels.[^cite_note-391][^cite_note-392][^cite_note-393] At least 143 people were killed in Gaza on 15 May, making it the deadliest day since the end of the ceasefire in March.[^cite_note-394] The following day, Israel announced the launch of [Operation Gideon's Chariots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Gideon%27s_Chariots), a military offensive aimed at taking control of the entire Gaza Strip. The move was condemned by several of Israel's allies, a number of whom threatened sanctions.[^cite_note-395][^cite_note-396][^cite_note-397] On 25 May, an [Israeli airstrike on the Fahmi al-Jawjawi school](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fahmi_al-Jarjawi_School_Massacre) in [Daraj](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daraj_Quarter), Gaza City, killed at least 36 people and injured over 55.[^cite_note-398] In late May a new militia in Gaza, the [Popular Forces](80146159.md), began operating under the authority of the Abu Shabab clan in opposition to Hamas. [Avigdor Lieberman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avigdor_Lieberman) accused Netanyahu and the Israeli government of funding and arming this militia.[^cite_note-haaretz20250605-336]&#x20;

On 27 May, the US-backed [Gaza Humanitarian Foundation](79923808.md) began operations in Tel al-Sultan to deliver humanitarian aid. As thousands of starving Palestinians overwhelmed the distribution center, Israeli forces fired into the crowd, killing ten and injuring at least 62 Palestinians.[^cite_note-399] In a series of subsequent [attacks on aid seekers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafah_aid_distribution_killings), more than 1,300 were killed.[^cite_note-400] On 30 June, an [Israeli airstrike on al-Baqa cafeteria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Baqa_Cafe_airstrike) killed at least 41 Palestinians—including photojournalist [Ismail Abu Hatab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ismail_Abu_Hatab)—and injured another 75.[^cite_note-401] On 20 July, the IDF issued evacuation orders for the city of [Deir al-Balah](1470463.md), where it had not launched a ground offensive since the start of the war.[^cite_note-402] On the following day, Israeli forces [advanced into the outskirts of Deir al-Balah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deir_al-Balah_offensive) as airstrikes hit the city.[^cite_note-403]&#x20;

In early August 2025, the Israeli security cabinet approved [a plan to occupy Gaza City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Gaza_City_offensive_plan). The plan drew condemnation from the UN, the European Council president, and several countries.[^cite_note-404][^cite_note-405] On 10 August, Israeli forces struck a press tent outside al-Shifa Hospital, killing six Al-Jazeera journalists including [Anas Al-Sharif](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anas_Al-Sharif),[^cite_note-406][^cite_note-407] bringing the number of journalists killed by Israel during the war to 192.[^cite_note-408] On 20 August, Israel began the first stages of the offensive, calling up 60,000 reservists for the beginning of September.[^cite_note-409][^cite_note-410] On 25 August, an Israeli [double tap strike on Nasser Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Nasser_Hospital_strikes) in southern Gaza killed 22 people, including five journalists.[^cite_note-411][^cite_note-412] Subsequently, Netanyahu said he "deeply regretted" the strike, describing it as a "mishap".[^cite_note-413] On 9 September, Israel ordered the full evacuation of Gaza City, citing plans for intensified operation.[^cite_note-414] Six days later, the IDF launched the full offensive, deploying units from two armored and infantry divisions.[^cite_note-415]&#x20;

### Third ceasefire (September 2025 – present)

For a chronological guide, see [Timeline of the Gaza war (3 October 2025 – present)](81320997.md).

Main articles: [Gaza peace plan](81215079.md) and [United Nations Security Council Resolution 2803](81623997.md)



Gaza peace plan phase one withdrawal line shown in yellow (the "[Yellow Line](81476537.md)")

On 29 September, US President [Donald Trump](4848272.md) proposed a [20-point peace plan](81215079.md), which included the release of all hostages, an end to the war, the withdrawal of Israel and the disarmament of Hamas, and a temporary transitional government.[^cite_note-pbs20250930-416] Israel accepted the deal, as did Arab and Muslim states who urged Hamas to agree to it.[^cite_note-417] On 3 October, under pressure from Trump,[^cite_note-418] Hamas agreed to release all hostages, end the war, and hand over administration of the Gaza Strip, though it wanted to continue negotiating.[^cite_note-419] The IDF halted the offensive in Gaza City on 4 October.[^cite_note-420] Both sides agreed to phase one of the peace plan on 9 October.[^cite_note-theguardian20251009-49] The following day, the IDF began a withdrawal from parts of the Gaza Strip; the ceasefire formally came into effect when this was completed at noon local time.[^cite_note-wsj20251010-50]&#x20;

[A peace summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Sharm_El_Sheikh_Summit) on 13 October in [Sharm El Sheikh](419335.md) was attended by representatives of over 30 countries and organizations.[^cite_note-421][^cite_note-422][^cite_note-423] A prisoner exchange took place, with Hamas releasing the remaining 20 living Israeli hostages,[^cite_note-424] while Israel released 1,718 Palestinian detainees held without charge and an additional 250 Palestinian prisoners convicted of crimes in Israeli courts.[^cite_note-425]&#x20;

Two IDF soldiers were killed in Rafah on 19 October; al-Qassam Brigades denied responsibility.[^cite_note-apnews20251019-426][^cite_note-427] Israel resumed bombardment of Gaza in response, killing at least 29 Palestinians before reaffirming the ceasefire the same day.[^cite_note-apnews20251019-426] On 28 October, one Israeli soldier was killed in Rafah; al-Qassam Brigades denied involvement.[^cite_note-apnews20251029-428][^cite_note-429] Netanyahu ordered Israeli forces to immediately conduct "powerful strikes" in Gaza:[^cite_note-430] the attacks killed at least 104 people. The IDF said it targeted dozens of Hamas targets, including 21 commanders and militants who participated in the 7 October attacks.[^cite_note-apnews20251029-428] On 13 December, an Israeli attack west of Gaza City killed five people and injured at least 25 others. It targeted the al-Qassam Brigades commander [Ra'ad Sa'ad](80190564.md), who was killed.[^cite_note-431][^cite_note-432]&#x20;

On 15 May 2026, Israeli strikes hit an apartment in Rimal area of Gaza City and a vehicle on a street in its vicinity, killing [Izz al-Din al-Haddad](79997859.md), the leader of Hamas in the Gaza Strip. Medics in Gaza said seven people were killed and at least 50 others were wounded.[^cite_note-433][^cite_note-434][^cite_note-435] An Israeli strike hit a building in Rimal area of Gaza City on 26 May, killing three people including [Mohammed Odeh](83210587.md), who had succeeded al-Haddad.[^cite_note-436][^cite_note-437][^cite_note-438]&#x20;

On 31 July, Trump said that his [Board of Peace](81274522.md) had reached an agreement to disarm Hamas and [other militant organisations in Gaza](75192468.md).[^cite_note-439][^cite_note-deccanchronicle20260731-52][^cite_note-haaretz20260731-53] Hamas leader [Ghazi Hamad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghazi_Hamad) stated that disarmament would be dependent on Israel's withdrawal from Gaza and progress on reconstruction, and that Hamas will not implement any part of the agreement unless Israeli forces fulfilled the terms of the agreement.[^cite_note-440][^cite_note-haaretz20260731-53] The [al-Quds Brigades](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Quds_Brigades) agreed to the disarmament plan on 2 August.[^cite_note-441] [Israel's political echelon](72545811.md) ordered the IDF not to launch strikes in Gaza without the authorization of IDF chief of staff Lt. Gen. [Eyal Zamir](61877756.md), following a US request to limit operations in Gaza.[^cite_note-442][^cite_note-443] In the run up to the [2026 Israeli elections](71244652.md), Netanyahu rejected the Board of Peace agreement.[^cite_note-444][^cite_note-445]&#x20;

## Impact

Main article: [Impacts of the Gaza war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Impacts_of_the_Gaza_war)

### Scale of destruction

Main article: [Israeli bombing of the Gaza Strip](76139149.md)



[Rimal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rimal) in [Gaza City](55387.md) following an Israeli airstrike, 10 October 2023

The scale and pace of [destruction and damage of buildings in the Gaza Strip](76139149.md) ranks among the most severe in modern history,[^cite_note-The_Washington_Post-2023-446][^cite_note-Wintour-2023-447][^cite_note-448] surpassing the level of destruction of [Dresden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bombing_of_Dresden) and [London](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Blitz) during [World War II](32927.md) and approaching that of [Hamburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bombing_of_Hamburg_in_World_War_II),[^cite_note-449][^cite_note-Dyer-2023-450][^cite_note-Malsin-2023-451] and included the destruction of apartment buildings, hospitals, schools, religious sites, factories, shopping centers, and municipal infrastructure.[^cite_note-Malsin-2023-451] As of January 2024, researchers at [Oregon State University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oregon_State_University) and the [City University of New York](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_University_of_New_York) estimated that 50–62% of buildings in the Gaza Strip had been damaged or destroyed.[^cite_note-452][^cite_note-453][^cite_note-455][^cite_note-457] The 29,000 munitions Israel had dropped on Gaza in three months exceeded the amount (3,678) dropped by the US between 2004 and 2010 after its [invasion of Iraq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Invasion_of_Iraq).[^cite_note-458] According to satellite analyses, 68% of roads, 70% of greenhouses, and nearly 70% of tree crops have been damaged or destroyed.[^cite_note-459] After a year, the UN estimates that 42m tonnes of rubble clutter the Strip; clearing and rebuilding might take 80 years and cost over \$80bn.[^cite_note-460]&#x20;

*The Guardian* reported that the scale of destruction has led international legal experts to raise the concept of [domicide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Domicide), which it describes as "the mass destruction of dwellings to make \[a] territory uninhabitable".[^cite_note-Wintour-2023-447] The term [urbicide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urbicide) has also been used to refer to the destruction of Gazan cities and their institutions.[^cite_note-461] In October 2024, [Forensic Architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forensic_Architecture) published a map platform detailing Israel's campaign in Gaza titled "A Cartography of Genocide", accompanied by a report that concludes that "Israel's military campaign in Gaza is organised, systematic, and intended to destroy conditions of life and life-sustaining infrastructure".[^cite_note-462] Psychologist [Daniel Bar-Tal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniel_Bar-Tal) attributed the scale of destruction in Gaza to the trauma and perceived existential threat produced by the [October 7 Hamas attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/October_7_Hamas_attack) and deterrence-oriented military objectives.[^cite_note-463]&#x20;

### Humanitarian crisis

Main article: [Gaza humanitarian crisis](75277791.md)

Further information: [Gaza Strip famine](75828824.md) and [Timeline of the Gaza Strip healthcare collapse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_the_Gaza_Strip_healthcare_collapse)

People stand amid the rubble of a building and looking at the ground. A man is carrying a large flower-patterned object.

Residents inspect the ruins of an apartment in Gaza destroyed by Israeli airstrikes

The Gaza Strip is experiencing a [humanitarian crisis](1251700.md) and a [healthcare collapse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_the_Gaza_Strip_healthcare_collapse) as a result of the war.[^cite_note-Ioanes-2023-464][^cite_note-Marsi-2023-465] At the start of the war, Israel tightened its [blockade](75013719.md), resulting in significant shortages of fuel, food, medication, water, and essential medical supplies.[^cite_note-Ioanes-2023-464][^cite_note-466][^cite_note-467] This siege resulted in a 90% drop in electricity availability, impacting hospital power supplies, sewage plants, and shutting down the desalination plants that provide drinking water.[^cite_note-468] In July 2024, available water was 4.74 litres per person per day, just under a third of the recommended minimum in emergencies.[^cite_note-469] Doctors warned of disease outbreaks spreading due to overcrowded hospitals.[^cite_note-Marsi-2023-465] A [polio epidemic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Gaza_Strip_polio_epidemic) was the target of mostly-successful vaccination campaigns.[^cite_note-470]&#x20;

Heavy bombardment by Israeli airstrikes caused catastrophic damage to Gaza's infrastructure, further deepening the crisis. Direct attacks on telecommunications infrastructure by Israel, electricity blockades, and fuel shortages caused the near-total collapse of Gaza's largest cell network providers.[^cite_note-Español-2024-471][^cite_note-Aly-2023-472][^cite_note-473] Lack of internet access has obstructed Gazan citizens from communicating with loved ones, learning of IDF operations, and identifying both the areas most exposed to bombing and possible escape routes.[^cite_note-Español-2024-471] The blackouts impeded emergency services, making it harder to locate and access the time-critical injured,[^cite_note-Español-2024-471] and have impeded humanitarian agencies and journalists.[^cite_note-Español-2024-471]&#x20;

In May 2025, Donald Trump acknowledged starvation in Gaza and backed the promoted [Gaza Humanitarian Foundation](79923808.md) to distribute food through hubs run by private contractors and protected by Israeli soldiers.[^cite_note-TheGuardian16May2025-474][^cite_note-CBSNews16May2025-475] International aid agencies warned that the plans weaponized humanitarian aid and would not be able to meet the scale of aid that was required.[^cite_note-476] UN humanitarian representatives [Cindy McCain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cindy_McCain) and Tom Fletcher noted that only about 100 trucks a day were getting through, compared to more that 600 trucks a day before the blockade.[^cite_note-CBSNews25May2025-477][^cite_note-BBC28July2025-478]&#x20;

Trump said Gaza is experiencing "real starvation", while Netanyahu blamed Hamas.[^cite_note-BBC28July2025-478][^cite_note-Guardian28July2025-479] In August 2025, an IPC analysis confirmed famine, or [IPC Phase 5](16431152.md), in some parts of Gaza including Gaza City.[^cite_note-480][^cite_note-ABCNews22August2025-481] This analysis predicted that a further 640,000 people would face Phase 5 conditions by September 2025.[^cite_note-ABCNews22August2025-481] [Coordinator of Government Activities in the Territories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coordinator_of_Government_Activities_in_the_Territories) issued a counter-report which it said refuted the contents of the IPC brief.[^cite_note-482] On 19 December 2025, the IPC released a report stating that famine conditions in Gaza no longer existed following improved food deliveries after the ceasefire, though the situation remained "fragile".[^cite_note-483]&#x20;

### Mass detention

Main article: [Mass detentions in the Gaza war](75234504.md)

Israel has increased its [administrative detention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Administrative_detention) of Palestinians and Palestinian citizens of Israel since the start of the war. Administrative detention was already at a 20-year high before October 2023.[^cite_note-484] More than 11,000 Palestinians are held in Israeli jails, not counting detainees taken from Gaza during the war.[^cite_note-thenewarab20241114-65] At least 60 Palestinians have died in Israeli detention since 7 October.[^cite_note-B'tselem-2024-08-05-485] They are held without charge or trial, which violates international law.[^cite_note-486]&#x20;

In December 2023, a military base at [Sde Teiman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sde_Teiman) in the Negev Desert was converted to a detention camp by the IDF. Whistleblowers and detainees reported torture of Palestinian detainees at the camp, as well as amputations of limbs due to injuries sustained from handcuffing, medical neglect, arbitrary punishment and sexual abuse. Prisoners have been coerced to confess that they are members of Hamas.[^cite_note-Fabian-2024-487][^cite_note-488][^cite_note-489] After conditions in the camp came to light in May 2024, the [Supreme Court of Israel](1666510.md) held a hearing and the IDF began transferring 1,200 of the prisoners to [Ofer Prison](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ofer_Prison).[^cite_note-490] Detainees have reported severe instances of violence during transfers between prisons.[^cite_note-B'tselem-2024-08-05-485][^cite_note-Human_Rights_Watch-2024-64] In July, ten soldiers "suspected of the serious sexual abuse" of a Palestinian detainee were arrested at Sde Teiman.[^cite_note-Fabian-2024-487] In protest, Israeli national security minister [Itamar Ben-Gvir](54504115.md) and other supporters of the arrested soldiers stormed both Sde Teiman and Beit Lid, where the soldiers were being held.[^cite_note-491]&#x20;

Several Palestinian healthcare workers have been abducted from Gaza hospitals during sieges by Israeli forces.[^cite_note-Human_Rights_Watch-2024-64] As of February 2025, at least 160 healthcare workers from Gaza were believed to be held in detention by Israel, with another 24 missing after being taken from hospitals in Gaza.[^cite_note-TheGuardian2802025-66] For example, in December 2024, Israeli forces abducted [Hussam Abu Safiya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hussam_Abu_Safiya), the director of Kamal Adwan Hospital, and 57 of the hospital's staff.[^cite_note-492]&#x20;

### Economic impact

Main article: [Economic impact of the Gaza war](75304309.md)

The war cost Israel 168 billion shekels (more than [US\$](18717338.md)50 billion) as of September 2025.[^cite_note-493] By September 2024 the US provided \$14.5 billion in aid, part of the \$22.76 billion the US had allocated for military assistance.[^cite_note-494][^cite_note-496] As early as 9 November 2023, the Bank of Israel reported that the drop in labor supply caused by the war was costing the Israeli economy \$600 million a week, or 6% of weekly GDP. The bank also stated that the estimate did not include damage caused by the absence of Palestinian and foreign workers.[^cite_note-497] In the final quarter of 2023, the Israeli economy shrank by 5.2% quarter-to-quarter due to labor shortages in construction and from the mobilization of 300,000 reservists.[^cite_note-498]&#x20;

According to UNCTAD's 2025 report, prolonged restrictions on movement, coupled with the war in October 2023, have resulted in significant long-term devastation in both Gaza and the West Bank. The per capita GDP has plummeted to \$161 annually and unemployment rates have soared to 80 percent.[^cite_note-499] It has been calculated that the [carbon cost](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carbon_price) in terms of climate impact of rebuilding Gaza would exceed the annual greenhouse emissions of 135 countries.[^cite_note-500]&#x20;

### Environmental impact

Further information: [Environmental impact of war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Environmental_impact_of_war) and [Environmental impact of the Gaza war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Environmental_impact_of_the_Gaza_war)

There has been extensive environmental damage caused by the Gaza war,[^cite_note-forensic-architecture-20240329-501] By March 2024, nearly half of the farmland in Gaza had been destroyed,[^cite_note-forensic-architecture-20240329-501][^cite_note-theguardian20240329-502] and by the following January 80% of the tree cover had been destroyed.[^cite_note-503]&#x20;



IDF bulldozer clearing trees in Gaza in October 2023

Israeli [bombardment](76139149.md) and the [blockade](75013719.md) have led to a total collapse of Gaza's civil infrastructure, including sewage treatment, waste disposal, water management, and fuel supplies. Water has been polluted by 130,000 cubic metres of sewage being discharged into the sea every day due to Israel cutting off fuel supplies. Groundwater has been contaminated by toxins and munitions and air has been polluted by smoke and particulates from bombing.[^cite_note-forensic-architecture-20240329-501][^cite_note-504] Soils have been degraded by uprooting trees and contaminated by toxins, munitions, heavy bombing and demolitions. Bombing by the Israeli army has created 50 million tonnes of debris and hazardous material, much of which contains human remains and tens of thousands of bombs. In June 2024, northern Gaza was described as a "wasteland", unable to sustain life.[^cite_note-505] The size of the damage have led to calls by the research group [Forensic Architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forensic_Architecture) at [Goldsmiths, University of London](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Goldsmiths,_University_of_London), and the [Palestinian Environmental NGOs Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_Environmental_NGOs_Network) for the Israeli Government to be investigated for the [Rome Statute](68762.md) war crime of [ecocide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ecocide) for "widespread, long-term and severe damage to the natural environment".[^cite_note-theguardian20240329-502]&#x20;

During the first nine months of war alone, 146 km<sup>2</sup> (56 sq mi) of vegetation in northern Israel and 8 square kilometers along the Gaza border was burned mostly by rocket fire from Gaza and Lebanon. This does not include the impact from drones and anti-tank guided missiles shot from Lebanon, nor fire from Yemen and Iraq. The fires increased [air pollution](10934212.md) accounting for 44% of [carbon monoxide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carbon_monoxide) emissions in Israel during the period, 13% of [fine particulate matter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fine_particulate_matter), 12% of particulate matter, 7% of non-methane [volatile organic compounds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volatile_organic_compound), and 1% of emissions from ammonia, anitrogen and sulfur oxides. The is 5 times more than the average area burned annually over the past decade in the Galilee and the Golan Heights. Before the war, multiple fires were sparked by [incendiary balloons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Incendiary_balloon) from the Gaza Strip. Due to the war, nine of 40 large companies close to the northern and Gaza borders failed to report their emissions as they are required by law. In the [Orot Rabin power station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orot_Rabin) the war led to operational changes creating a 24% increase in emissions of large particulate matter in 2023 compared to 2022. A crane collapse in southern Ashdod last year also contributed to the rise. In the Rutenberg power plant the war led to release of 728 tons of nitrogen oxides into the atmosphere, and in the Ashdod oil refinery 290 tons sulfur oxides.[^cite_note-506] By the end of 2024, 340 km<sup>2</sup> (130 sq mi) of [woodland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Woodland) were burned mostly by Hezbollah rockets.[^cite_note-507] Considering agricultural land, in the southern part of the western Negev, crops were burnt. The war with Hezbollah burned a larger area - about 20,000 dunams. Wildlife also suffered greatly. Soil became heavily degraded.[^cite_note-508]&#x20;

## Casualties

Main article: [Casualties of the Gaza war](75130785.md)

| Event | Total | Civilians | Children |  |  |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Total | % | Total | % |  |  |
| [October 7 attacks](74998317.md) | 1,195[^cite_note-hrw20240717-509] | 828[^cite_note-hrw20240717-509][^cite_note-timesofisrael20240806-510] | 69.2%[^cite_note-511] | 36[^cite_note-social_security_data-512] | 3.01%[^cite_note-513] |
| Israeli invasion and bombing of Gaza | 73,922[^cite_note-WHO-HealthCluster-Dashboard-514] | \~59,031[^cite_note-515] | \~80%[^cite_note-aoav20240802-516][^cite_note-thenation20240530-517] | 21,638[^cite_note-WHO-HealthCluster-Dashboard-514] | 29.27%[^cite_note-518] |
| [Israeli attacks in the West Bank](75337172.md) | 1,209[^cite_note-WHO-HealthCluster-Dashboard-514] |  |  | 254[^cite_note-WHO-HealthCluster-Dashboard-514] | 21.01%[^cite_note-519] |

\
&#x20;As of 27 September 2026, at least 77,271 people (75,225+ Palestinians[^cite_note-520] and 2,046+ Israelis)[^cite_note-defense-ministry-data-26][^cite_note-israeli-civilian-casualties-24][^cite_note-wp-hostagecount-27][^cite_note-ap-74000-521] have been reported killed in the Gaza war according to the [Gaza Health Ministry](52186562.md) (GHM) and Israeli Ministry of Foreign Affairs, including [270 journalists and media workers](75146557.md),[^cite_note-522] 120 academics,[^cite_note-523] and over 560 humanitarian aid workers,[^cite_note-524] a number that includes 393 employees of [UNRWA](32270.md).[^cite_note-525] A study by [OHCHR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OHCHR) found that 70% of the Palestinians killed in residential buildings or similar housing were [women](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Women_in_the_Gaza_war) and [children](75987631.md).[^cite_note-axios20241108-309][^cite_note-ohchr-report-20241108-526]&#x20;

In January 2025, an analysis of deaths between October 2023 and 30 June 2024 estimated 64,260 deaths from traumatic injury during this period, and likely exceeding 70,000 by October 2024, with 59.1% of them being women, children and the elderly. It concluded that the GHM undercounted trauma-related deaths by 41% in its report, and also noted that its findings "do not account for non-trauma-related deaths."[^cite_note-Lancet2025-36] A comparable estimate up to May 2025 would be 93,000 (77,000 to 109,000), representing 4–5% of Gaza's pre-war population.[^cite_note-economist20250508-527] Another casualty analysis, published in February 2026, estimated 75,200 violent deaths and 8,540 excess non-violent deaths between 7 October 2023 and 5 January 2025. The estimate of violent deaths is 34.7% higher than the GHM's casualty count at the time. Of the violent deaths, the researchers estimate that 56.2% were women, children, and elderly individuals.[^cite_note-528]&#x20;

A May 2025 investigation found that a classified IDF database listed 8,900 Palestinian fighters as dead or likely dead, or 17% of the 53,000 death toll at the time, which if correct would indicate that 83% of the dead were civilians—among conflicts since 1989, only the [Srebrenica massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Srebrenica_massacre) (though not the [Bosnian war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bosnian_war) overall), the [siege of Mariupol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_of_Mariupol), and the [Rwandan genocide](676659.md) have seen a higher rate of civilian casualties.[^cite_note-theguardian20250821-529] In response, the IDF said that the numbers in the article are incorrect.[^cite_note-530]&#x20;

A survey by the [Palestinian Center for Policy and Survey Research](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_Center_for_Policy_and_Survey_Research) (PCPSR) reported showed over 60% of Gazans have lost family members since the war began.[^cite_note-531][^cite_note-532] Thousands of more dead bodies are thought to be under the rubble of destroyed buildings.[^cite_note-533][^cite_note-534] The number of injured is greater than 100,000;[^cite_note-535] United Nations agencies have reported that Gaza is now home to the highest number of child amputees per capita in the world.[^cite_note-536]&#x20;

The majority of casualties have been in the [Gaza Strip](12047.md). The GHM total casualty count does not distinguish Palestinian deaths by Israeli attacks from those by other means, like friendly fire, and describes all casualties as victims of "Israeli aggression".[^cite_note-537] On 3 March 2026, the GHM published the names, gender and birth date of 72,004 individual Palestinians whose identities were confirmed and continues to attempt to identify all casualties.[^cite_note-haaretz20260303-538]&#x20;

The [October 7 attacks](74998317.md) on Israel killed 1,195 people, including 815 civilians.[^cite_note-hrw20240717-509][^cite_note-539] Casualties have also occurred in other parts of Israel, as well as in southern [Lebanon](17771.md),[^cite_note-540] [Syria](7515849.md),[^cite_note-541] [Yemen](350939.md),[^cite_note-542] and [Iran](14653.md).[^cite_note-543] As reported by the Defense Ministry, the total number of Israeli fatalities as of October 2025 is 1,152. This figure encompasses soldiers, police personnel, Shin Bet operatives, and civilian security officials who died on duty, as well as those who succumbed to accidents, illness, or suicide. Notably, around 42% of the deceased were individuals under the age of 21.[^cite_note-544] According to the Israeli Ministry of Defense's Rehabilitation Division, about 1,000 soldiers are wounded every month.[^cite_note-545] In August 2024, the ministry predicted that it would have to account for 100,000 disabled IDF veterans by 2030 due to the war.[^cite_note-546] An August 2025 report indicated that the rise in suicides among IDF soldiers was due to psychological trauma resulting from the prolonged conflict.[^cite_note-547]&#x20;

## War crimes

Main article: [War crimes in the Gaza war](75113830.md)

See also: [Palestinian war crimes](76545817.md) and [Israeli war crimes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_war_crimes)

In the aftermath of the October 7 attack, a [UN Commission to the Israel–Palestine conflict](69971286.md) stated that there is "clear evidence that war crimes may have been committed".[^cite_note-UN11-548][^cite_note-ohchr-549] The [International Criminal Court](14880.md) (ICC) noted that its [mandate to investigate allegations of war crimes in Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Criminal_Court_investigation_in_Palestine) extended to the current conflict.[^cite_note-UNHRC-1210-550][^cite_note-551] On 21 November, the ICC issued arrest warrants for Netanyahu, Gallant, and Hamas leader [Mohammed Deif](92534.md), for alleged war crimes and [crimes against humanity](61099.md) during the war;[^cite_note-552][^cite_note-553][^cite_note-554] Deif's arrest warrant was cancelled after his death.[^cite_note-555]&#x20;

In June 2024, the UN commission published a [report covering the war from 7 October to 31 December 2023](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diplomatic_impact_of_the_Gaza_war#Independent_International_Commission_of_Inquiry_on_the_Occupied_Palestinian_Territory_report).[^cite_note-556] The report found that Hamas and six other Palestinian groups were responsible for the war crimes of intentionally directing attacks against civilians, murder or willful killing, torture, inhuman or cruel treatment, destroying or seizing property, outrages upon personal dignity, and taking hostages, including children.[^cite_note-557][^cite_note-558] The commission also concluded that Israeli authorities were responsible for the war crimes of starvation as a method of warfare, murder or willful killing, intentionally directing attacks against civilians and civilian objects, forcible transfer, sexual violence, torture and inhuman or cruel treatment, arbitrary detention and outrages upon personal dignity. It also found that Israel had committed crimes against humanity, including carrying out the extermination of Palestinians and gender persecution targeting Palestinian men and boys.[^cite_note-559][^cite_note-560][^cite_note-561] The commission said that they had submitted 7,000 pieces of evidence of crimes committed by Israel and Hamas to the ICC.[^cite_note-562]&#x20;

In another report published in October 2024, the commission accused the IDF of war crimes through deliberately killing and torturing medical personnel, targeting medical vehicles, and restricting patients from leaving Gaza. The report also found that thousands of Palestinian prisoners, many arbitrarily detained, faced widespread abuse, including violence, rape and sexual assault, and torture, and highlighted that resulting deaths constituted war crimes and violations of the right to life. Israel refused to cooperate with the investigation, contending that it had an "anti-Israel" bias.[^cite_note-563][^cite_note-564] In July 2024, five [IDF](39237.md) soldiers gang raped a [Palestinian](23267.md) detainee from Gaza, which was partially caught on [cctv](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cctv). Due to "exceptional circumstances", the charges for the soldiers were dropped. The victim was stabbed in the rectum, and suffered severe injuries to the lungs and ribs.[^cite_note-565][^cite_note-566][^cite_note-567]&#x20;
