Main article: [Gaza genocide](75668427.md)

See also: [Allegations of genocide in the October 7 attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allegations_of_genocide_in_the_October_7_attacks)

In December 2023, the government of [South Africa](17416221.md) instituted proceedings, *[South Africa v. Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa_v._Israel)*, against Israel at the [International Court of Justice](14918.md) (ICJ), alleging a violation of the [Genocide Convention](143988.md).[^cite_note-ICJ-2023-12-29-568] In January 2024, the court ordered Israel to take all measures within its power to prevent the commission of acts of genocide, to prevent and punish [incitement to genocide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Incitement_to_genocide), and to allow basic humanitarian service, aid and supplies into Gaza.[^cite_note-ICJruling-569][^cite_note-570][^cite_note-571] Israel did not fully comply with the court's orders.[^cite_note-Amnesty_International_Blockade-572][^cite_note-Human_Rights_Watch_Blockade-573][^cite_note-574]&#x20;

In December 2024, [Amnesty International](18947898.md) concluded that Israel was committing [genocide](12441.md) against Palestinians in the Gaza Strip;[^cite_note-amnesty20241205-59][^cite_note-575] [Human Rights Watch](46530.md) also concluded that month that Israel is responsible for the crime of genocide through deprivation of safe water and sanitation.[^cite_note-576] In March 2025, the UN Commission found that Israel's attacks on women's healthcare facilities in Gaza amounted to genocidal acts;[^cite_note-577] Israeli prime minister Benjamin Netanyahu condemned the report as "false and absurd", accusing the UN Human Rights Council of being anti-Israel and anti-Semitic.[^cite_note-578] In July 2025, Israeli human rights groups [B'Tselem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B%27Tselem)[^cite_note-B'Tselem_2025-579] and [Physicians for Human Rights–Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Physicians_for_Human_Rights%E2%80%93Israel)[^cite_note-PHR_Jul_2025-580] released reports calling Israel's campaign in Gaza a genocide.[^cite_note-B'Tselem_2025-579][^cite_note-Kottasová_&_Salman-581]&#x20;

On 16 September 2025, the [UN Commission of Inquiry on the Occupied Palestinian Territory](69971286.md) concluded that Israel had [committed genocide against Palestinians in Gaza](75668427.md), and that top Israeli officials including Netanyahu, Gallant, and Herzog had incited these acts. The report said that four of the five acts of genocide have been committed, as defined under the 1948 Genocide Convention. Israel rejected the report as distorted and false, and called for the abolition of the Commission.[^cite_note-un_coi-582][^cite_note-un_coi_cnn-44]&#x20;

The genocide has also been recognized by [a United Nations special committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_Committee_to_Investigate_Israeli_Practices_Affecting_the_Human_Rights_of_the_Palestinian_People)[^cite_note-UNCommitteeHeadGenocide-583] and the [International Association of Genocide Scholars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Association_of_Genocide_Scholars),[^cite_note-IAGSReuters-584][^cite_note-IAGSResolution-585] numerous [genocide studies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genocide_studies) and [international law](8195726.md) scholars,[^cite_note-growing_agreement-586][^cite_note-genocide_int_law_scholars-41] and other experts.[^cite_note-587][^cite_note-588]&#x20;

In June 2026, *[The Guardian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Guardian)* reported that a UN Commission of Inquiry stated that Palestinian children had been deliberately targeted during the conflict, and that children accounted for approximately 30% of reported fatalities. The report said this pattern was relevant to its assessment of genocidal intent.[^cite_note-589][^cite_note-un_coi-582]&#x20;

## Spillover

Main article: [Middle Eastern crisis (2023–present)](75895273.md)







- Rising smoke after the [Israel strike on Hezbollah headquarters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Hezbollah_headquarters_strike)

- A damaged building in Kiryat Shmona, northern Israel

- Two United States [carrier strike groups](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carrier_strike_group) in the [Mediterranean Sea](19006.md).

The war's spillover resulted in a [major escalation](76694649.md) of [existing tensions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran%E2%80%93Israel_proxy_conflict) between Israel and Iran, with groups in the [Axis of Resistance](36658251.md) launching [attacks on American military bases](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attacks_on_U.S._bases_in_Iraq,_Jordan,_and_Syria_during_the_Israel%E2%80%93Hamas_war), and the Yemeni [Houthi movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthi_movement) [attacking](75207766.md) commercial vessels in the [Red Sea](26191.md) and incurring a US-led [military operation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Prosperity_Guardian).[^cite_note-590] Hezbollah in southern Lebanon and the Houthi movement launched attacks against Israel shortly after the start of the war. Iranian-backed militias in Iraq and Syria have also traded attacks with the US and IDF.[^cite_note-591]&#x20;

Israel has bombed targets in and around [Damascus](8914.md) throughout the war,[^cite_note-Reuters-2023-592][^cite_note-593][^cite_note-AP_News-2024-594] with an [attack on the Iranian embassy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_airstrike_on_the_Iranian_consulate_in_Damascus) in Damascus on 1 April 2024 leading to a series of [retaliatory airstrikes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/April_2024_Iranian_strikes_against_Israel) on Israel.[^cite_note-Tanyos-2024-595][^cite_note-CNN-2024-596] On 31 July, Hamas political leader Ismail Haniyeh [was assassinated](77478235.md) in [Tehran](57654.md),[^cite_note-597] and on 1 October, [Iran fired approximately 200 missiles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/October_2024_Iranian_strikes_against_Israel) at Israel.[^cite_note-theguardian20241001-598][^cite_note-Schneider-2024-599]&#x20;

By the end of 2024, a year-long [exchange of strikes](75005425.md) between Israel and Hezbollah escalated into an [Israeli invasion of Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Israeli_invasion_of_Lebanon), before it was paused after a [ceasefire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Israel%E2%80%93Lebanon_ceasefire_agreement).[^cite_note-APIH-600] In February 2025, Israel largely withdrew but maintained five military outposts on highlands in Southern Lebanon, against Lebanon's wishes.[^cite_note-601][^cite_note-602] The crisis has also seen [the fall](78548066.md) of the [Assad regime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assad_regime) and an ongoing [Israeli invasion of Syria](78549398.md).[^cite_note-603][^cite_note-604] Israeli defense minister Israel Katz in April 2025 declared that Israeli forces would indefinitely remain in "security zones" that they "cleared and seized" in Gaza, Lebanon and Syria.[^cite_note-605]&#x20;

On 13 June 2025, the [Twelve-Day War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twelve-Day_War) began after Israel launched large-scale attacks against targets in several areas in Iran, killing Iran's top military leadership,[^cite_note-606] including [Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps](1285472.md) (IRGC) commander [Hossein Salami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hossein_Salami),[^cite_note-607] Iranian Armed Forces Chief of Staff Major General [Mohammad Bagheri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammad_Bagheri_\(general\)),[^cite_note-608] and nuclear scientists [Fereydoon Abbasi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fereydoon_Abbasi) and [Mohammad Mehdi Tehranchi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammad_Mehdi_Tehranchi) were killed in the attacks, according to Iranian state media.[^cite_note-609] The [United States joined Israel in bombing Iran](80262844.md) on 22 June 2025, saying to have destroyed several nuclear sites.[^cite_note-610]&#x20;

On 9 September 2025, [Israel bombed the Hamas headquarters in Qatar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_attack_on_Doha), reportedly targeting several members of the [Hamas temporary committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamas_temporary_committee), including [Khalil al-Hayya](43349601.md), [Khaled Mashal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khaled_Mashal), [Muhammad Ismail Darwish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhammad_Ismail_Darwish), [Mousa Abu Marzook](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mousa_Abu_Marzook), and [Zaher Jabarin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zaher_Jabarin).[^cite_note-NYT20250909-611][^cite_note-TOI2025-612] The attack failed to assassinate the Hamas leadership in Qatar.[^cite_note-613] The stated Israeli reason for the bombing was the [Ramot Junction shooting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramot_Junction_shooting) in East Jerusalem the day prior.[^cite_note-614]&#x20;

In February 2026, the United States and Israel launched a [major war on Iran](82533582.md).[^cite_note-nytimes20260228-77]&#x20;

### West Bank and Israel

Further information: [Israeli incursions in the West Bank during the Gaza war](75337172.md)



West Bank sector of war\


&#x20; West Bank [Palestinian enclaves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_enclaves) ([Areas A & B](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Bank_Areas_in_the_Oslo_II_Accord))

&#x20; West Bank under Israeli control ([Area C](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Area_C))

&#x20; Israeli-annexed [East Jerusalem](343508.md)

Violence in the West Bank increased from the beginning of the war, with more than 607 Palestinians and over 25 Israelis killed by August 2024.[^cite_note-615][^cite_note-616] At the same time, [Israeli settler violence](47246246.md) further increased to around 1,270 attacks, against 856 for all of 2022.[^cite_note-Knell-Luckhurst-2024-617] In the weeks after the 7 October attack, about 1,000 Palestinians were forcibly displaced by settlers and Israeli forces,[^cite_note-618] and six Palestinian communities were abandoned.[^cite_note-619] On 19 October, more than 60 Hamas members were arrested and 13 people were killed in overnight [Israeli raids](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/October_2023_Tulkarm_raid) across the West Bank.[^cite_note-620][^cite_note-621]&#x20;

In July 2024, Israeli authorities approved the seizure of 12.7 square kilometers in the West Bank, the largest single land seizure since the 1993 Oslo accords.[^cite_note-APWB-622] Israeli authorities also approved plans for almost 5,300 new houses in occupied West Bank.[^cite_note-623] By this point, Israeli land seizures exceeded the combined total of the previous 20 years.[^cite_note-624] The following month, the Israeli government approved new settlements in the area.[^cite_note-625][^cite_note-626]&#x20;

On 7 August, [Wafa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wafa) reported that Israeli forces destroyed the regional headquarters of [Fatah](11104.md) in the [Balata Camp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Balata_Camp).[^cite_note-627][^cite_note-628] On 28 August, Israel launched [the largest military operation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Israeli_military_operation_in_the_northern_West_Bank) into the northern West Bank in more than 20 years. Israeli Foreign Minister [Israel Katz](19041378.md) called the operation a "full-fledged war".[^cite_note-629] Israeli forces carried out simultaneous operations in Jenin, Tubas, Nablus, Ramallah and Tulkarm.[^cite_note-630] The IDF classified the West Bank as a "combat zone" in early September, viewing it as the second most important front in the war.[^cite_note-631][^cite_note-632] Yoav Gallant said that Israel was "[mowing the lawn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mowing_the_lawn)" with its West Bank operations, but that it would eventually need to "pull out the roots".[^cite_note-633] On 6 September, Turkish-American protestor [Ayşenur Eygi was killed by an Israeli sniper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Killing_of_Aysenur_Eygi) at a demonstration near Nablus.[^cite_note-634]&#x20;

On 3 October, [an Israeli airstrike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tulkarm_camp_airstrike) in [Tulkarm Camp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tulkarm_Camp) killed at least 20 people.[^cite_note-635][^cite_note-636] On 13 November, Israeli finance minister [Bezalel Smotrich](46196834.md) said that with Trump's victory in the [2024 United States presidential election](59568546.md), Israel was "a step away" from "sovereignty in Judea and Samaria," with later comments by [the future US ambassador to Israel](301237.md) corroborating the possibility of annexation of the West Bank.[^cite_note-637] On 21 January 2025, the IDF launched a major [raid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Israeli_operation_in_the_West_Bank) in the West Bank.[^cite_note-638][^cite_note-639] On 8 September, two Hamas militants [opened fire inside a bus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Ramot_Junction_shooting), killing six Israelis and injuring 21 in [East Jerusalem](343508.md).[^cite_note-640]&#x20;

On 30 November 2023, two Palestinian gunmen [killed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Givat_Shaul_shooting) three and wounded 11 Israeli civilians at a bus stop on the [Givat Shaul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Givat_Shaul) Interchange in Jerusalem. Hamas claimed responsibility.[^cite_note-641] On 16 February 2024, a Palestinian gunman [shot and killed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Kiryat_Malakhi_attack) two Israeli civilians and injured four others in [Kiryat Malakhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kiryat_Malakhi), Israel. The shooter was killed by an off-duty IDF reservist at the scene.[^cite_note-642] On 18 August, a Hamas militant from Nablus carried out [a suicide bombing in Tel Aviv](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lehi_Street_bombing), wounding one person.[^cite_note-643] On 1 October, two Hamas gunmen from Hebron carried out [a shooting attack in Jaffa, Tel Aviv](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Jaffa_shooting), killing seven people and injuring 17 others.[^cite_note-644]&#x20;

### American involvement

Further information: [United States support for Israel in the Gaza war](65284610.md) and [2025 Donald Trump Gaza Strip takeover proposal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Donald_Trump_Gaza_Strip_takeover_proposal)







- US Air Force aircraft [carrying ammunition to Israel](65284610.md) on October 15, 2023

- US President [Joe Biden](145422.md) on a support visit to Israeli Prime Minister [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) in Tel Aviv on October 18, 2023

- US President [Donald Trump](4848272.md) receiving Israeli Prime Minister Benjamin Netanyahu at the White House on April 7, 2025.

Since 7 October, [the United States has aided Israel](65284610.md) in pursuing its war goals in Gaza as well as attempting to deter Hamas's allies in the region and to prevent them from expanding the scope of confrontation with Israel in the Middle East.[^cite_note-Chamas2024-645]<sup>: 67 </sup> The extent of American support for Israel has surpassed its involvement in all previous Israeli wars, leading [Gilbert Achcar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gilbert_Achcar) to call it 'the first US-Israeli joint war'.[^cite_note-646] Similarly, French historian Jean-Pierre Filiu argued that the extent of American support and involvement for Israel in its war against Palestine makes it a co-belligerent.[^cite_note-647] The United States has funded 70% of the total costs of the Gaza war since October 2023. The true level of US investment is higher, as this figure does not include its aid to Egypt and Jordan, which is intended to maintain their peace treaties with Israel.[^cite_note-648] Israel was also allowed to supply itself from American weapon stockpiles located in Israel.[^cite_note-Chamas2024-645]<sup>: 70 </sup>&#x20;

Alongside substantial financial and diplomatic support, the US also intervened in the war directly. 100 American soldiers were deployed in combat to man a [THAAD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/THAAD) anti-air battery.[^cite_note-telegraph20241015-649][^cite_note-nytimes20241013-650] In addition, the US piloted drones over Gaza to provide intelligence to Israel,[^cite_note-651] aimed at locating hostages and Palestinian militant leaders in Gaza;[^cite_note-652] this also included information on Sinwar's location.[^cite_note-653] During the Biden administration, the US sent a Marine three-star general and other U.S. military officers to Israel to advise on its operations in Gaza.[^cite_note-654] In November 2023, US special forces and commandos were deployed to help Israel track down hostages held in Gaza.[^cite_note-655] On 18 March 2025, after Israel's surprise attack on the Gaza Strip, Israeli government spokesman [David Mencer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Mencer) stated that the operation had been "fully coordinated with Washington" and thanked the [Trump administration](78298108.md) "for their unyielding support for Israel".[^cite_note-656] On 6 April, a second THAAD system was deployed to Israel by the US.[^cite_note-657]&#x20;

During negotiations for an end to the war, American officials said there were plans to send 200 US soldiers to Israel to oversee the implementation of the ceasefire, though they said they would not enter Gaza, but instead Central Command will establish a "civil-military coordination center".[^cite_note-658]&#x20;

### Diplomatic impact

Main articles: [Diplomatic impact of the Gaza war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diplomatic_impact_of_the_Gaza_war) and [International reactions to the Gaza war](75001640.md)



US Secretary of State [Antony Blinken](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antony_Blinken) and foreign ministers of the [Gulf Cooperation Council](220950.md) member states in Riyadh, Saudi Arabia, 19 April 2024

The war sparked a [diplomatic crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diplomatic_crisis) and affected the momentum of regional relations.[^cite_note-659] Negotiations have focused on the possibility of a ceasefire, with the US, [Egypt](8087628.md) and [Qatar](8238258.md) serving as mediators.[^cite_note-660][^cite_note-661] The [United Nations Security Council](31956.md) passed [resolution 2728](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_Resolution_2728) in March 2024, calling for an immediate ceasefire,[^cite_note-aljazeera20240325-268][^cite_note-662] and [resolution 2735](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_Resolution_2735) in June 2024, calling for acceptance of the [three-phase ceasefire proposal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/January_2025_Gaza_war_ceasefire).[^cite_note-663]&#x20;



U.S. Vice President [Kamala Harris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamala_Harris) with Israeli President [Isaac Herzog](4645588.md) at the [60th Munich Security Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/60th_Munich_Security_Conference) in Germany, February 2024

Significant geopolitical divisions emerged during the war. Israel's traditional [Western](21208200.md) allies provided diplomatic backing and weaponry to Israel;[^cite_note-Busari-2024-664] including the United States,[^cite_note-665] United Kingdom,[^cite_note-Chuter-2023-666] and Germany.[^cite_note-667] At least 44 nations denounced Hamas and explicitly condemned its conduct on 7 October as terrorism, including a joint statement by the US, UK, France, Italy, and Germany.[^cite_note-WI-Waldo-668]&#x20;

Several European nations have been less supportive of Israel's actions, most notably [Spain](26667.md), [Norway](21241.md), and [Ireland](14560.md), all of which formally [recognized the State of Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_recognition_of_Palestine) in a coordinated move in June 2024.[^cite_note-669] Spain and Ireland have also supported [South Africa's genocide case against Israel](75736183.md).[^cite_note-670][^cite_note-671] This has led to retaliatory action by Israel, who recalled its ambassadors to all three countries and later announced that it would be closing its embassy in Dublin.[^cite_note-672][^cite_note-673][^cite_note-674]&#x20;

The [Islamic world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_world) and much of the [Global South](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_South) denounced the actions of Israel and its allies, criticizing the "moral authority of the West" and alleging that it holds [double standards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Double_standard) surrounding [human rights](13831.md),[^cite_note-Busari-2024-664][^cite_note-Phillips-2023-675] comparing the West's unanimous condemnation of [Russia's 2022 invasion of Ukraine](79400268.md) to its implicit or explicit support of Israel's occupation of Palestine.[^cite_note-676] At least nine countries withdrew their ambassadors or cut diplomatic ties with Israel.[^cite_note-677] Global public opinion of Israel dropped during the war; a Morning Consult poll published in January 2024 indicated that the United States was the only remaining wealthy country in which Israel had net positive approval.[^cite_note-d904-678] The Israeli government's response prompted international [protests](75044900.md), arrests, and [harassment](75087530.md).[^cite_note-679]&#x20;



Pro-Palestinian protest in [Helsinki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Helsinki), Finland, 28 October 2023

&#x20;On 21 September 2025, the United Kingdom, Australia, Canada, and Portugal formally [recognised](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_recognition_of_Palestine) a Palestinian state.[^cite_note-680] France, Luxembourg, Monaco, Malta, and others did so the following day.[^cite_note-681]

## Peace plan

Main article: [Gaza peace plan](81215079.md)

Following the US-backed ceasefire proposal's implementation in October 2025, a broader peace plan has been advanced by the Trump administration. Following the prisoner exchanges that took place in mid-October, the plan calls for the division of Gaza into an Israeli-controlled outer zone and a coastal region to be occupied by a US-led international force before power is transferred to Palestinian technocrats under the supervision of a "Board of Peace" chaired by Trump.[^cite_note-mondediplonovember2025-682] The [United Nations Security Council](31956.md) has authorized the [International Stabilization Force](81225292.md) (ISF) and the [Board of Peace](81274522.md) to operate in Gaza until 2027.[^cite_note-683] The plan calls for the disarmament of Hamas and demilitarization of the Gaza Strip,[^cite_note-684][^cite_note-685][^cite_note-pbs20250930-416][^cite_note-686] and the creation of a [special economic zone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_economic_zone). The reconstruction of Gaza is estimated to cost \$70 billion, and had "no modern comparison" according to a Brookings Institution researcher.[^cite_note-687] The details of the proposal have been contentious, with Hamas insisting on Palestinian governance being "based on Palestinian national consensus"[^cite_note-mondediplonovember2025-682] and rejecting disarmament in the absence of broader political and security guarantees, among other conditions set out in US proposals.[^cite_note-688][^cite_note-689][^cite_note-690] The ceasefire has been violated by both sides.[^cite_note-691][^cite_note-692] According to *[Daily News Egypt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daily_News_Egypt)*, Israeli ceasefire violations have complicated the broader peace plan's implementation,[^cite_note-693] with continued Israeli strikes in Gaza killing more than 1,000 Palestinians since the start of the ceasefire (both fighters and civilians).[^cite_note-694] In May 2026 [Nickolay Mladenov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nickolay_Mladenov), high representative for Gaza to the Board of Peace, said that the phased plan was paralyzed over Hamas not disarming.[^cite_note-695]&#x20;

In December 2025, IDF Chief of Staff [Eyal Zamir](61877756.md) said that the Yellow Line would be Israel's new border line in the Gaza Strip,[^cite_note-696] despite the Gaza peace plan's stipulation that Israel will not occupy or annex Gaza.[^cite_note-697] Zamir said that Israel would remain in operational control over territory beyond the Yellow Line, which amounts to 53% of the Gaza Strip.[^cite_note-698] Israel has moved the Yellow Line further into Gaza[^cite_note-699][^cite_note-700] and applies a shoot-to-kill policy toward anyone approaching or crossing the line.[^cite_note-701][^cite_note-702][^cite_note-703] Within parts of Gaza, the Yellow Line is not clearly marked or delineated.[^cite_note-704] The Trump administration has said that it plans to build prefabricated structures on Israel's side of the Yellow Line; the plan was met with skepticism from Israeli officials and Arab diplomats. Arab diplomats have expressed reluctance to deploy ISF troops on the Palestinian side of the Yellow Line, suggesting that it would be better to deploy the ISF in lieu of Israeli soldiers first.[^cite_note-705] An analysis by [Forensic Architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forensic_Architecture) and [Drop Site News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drop_Site_News) showed that Israel had built 13 new outposts in Gaza since the start of the ceasefire. Palestinian property in eastern Khan Younis was systematically destroyed in areas where new military infrastructure was built.[^cite_note-706] On 24 December, Defense Minister Israel Katz suggested that Israel would build settlements in Gaza, but later walked back his comments.[^cite_note-707] In a joint statement on Israeli recognition of Somaliland, several Arab and Muslim states including Saudi Arabia, Egypt, Jordan and Turkey expressed concern that Israel intended to expel the Palestinian population in Gaza and resettle Palestinians in East Africa.[^cite_note-708]&#x20;

## Public approval

See also: [Gaza war protests in Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_war_protests_in_Israel)

Shortly after the October 7 attacks, there was broad support in Israeli society for military operations in Gaza.[^cite_note-709][^cite_note-710] A public opinion poll conducted in December 2023 by the [Israel Democracy Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_Democracy_Institute) found that 87% of Jewish Israelis supported the war in Gaza.[^cite_note-711] In another Israel Democracy Institute survey of 510 Israeli citizens in early February 2024, 68% of respondents supported preventing all international aid from entering Gaza.[^cite_note-712] A poll commissioned by [Penn State University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Penn_State_University) and conducted in March 2025 among a representative sample of 1,005 Israeli Jews found that 82% supported the forced expulsion of Gaza residents. Additionally, 47% responded affirmatively to the question: "When conquering an enemy city, should the IDF act like the Israelites led by Joshua when they [conquered Jericho](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fall_of_Jericho), that is, kill all its inhabitants?"[^cite_note-713][^cite_note-714]&#x20;

Throughout the war, [multiple protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_war_protests_in_Israel) have been held in Israel, primarily to call for the government to secure the return of hostages held by Hamas through ceasefire negotiations, and to protest the government's failure to anticipate the October 7 attacks. By September 2025, weekly protests had taken place in Tel Aviv and other locations in Israel.[^cite_note-715] The Knesset's law criminalizing "terrorist materials" consumption drew criticism.[^cite_note-716]&#x20;

A December 2023 poll by PCPSR found that 72% of respondents (52% of Gazans and 85% of West Bank residents) approved of the October 7 attacks.[^cite_note-717] A 2024 follow-up poll found that two-thirds of respondents continued to approve of the attacks.[^cite_note-stern-718] About 90% of respondents did not believe that Hamas committed atrocities (killing women and children, sexual violence) during the October 7 attacks, seeing this as enemy propaganda.[^cite_note-stern-718] Half of respondents in Gaza expected Hamas to win the war, while a quarter expected Israel to win.[^cite_note-stern-718]&#x20;

A 2025 Gallup poll found that 60% of Americans disapproved of Israel's military actions in Gaza while 32% supported them. Younger adults and Democrats were much more likely to oppose Israel's actions than Republicans. A 2025 Pew Research Center poll found that 39% of Americans believed that Israel's actions had been excessive in Gaza and approximately 59% held a poor view of Israel's actions in the war.[^cite_note-719]&#x20;

International public opinion surveys generally recorded increasingly negatively views of Israel as the war continued. A June 2026 Pew Research Center survey across 36 countries found that majorities or pluralities in most countries held unfavorable views of Israel, while confidence in Israel's Prime Minister Benjamin Netanyahu was also low across many of the countries surveyed. Unfavorable views were particularly more common among younger adults and those identifying with the political left.[^cite_note-720]&#x20;

## Media coverage

Main article: [Media coverage of the Gaza war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media_coverage_of_the_Gaza_war)

In reporting on the conflict, foreign media have limited access to Gaza and only in the presence of Israeli soldiers. *[Vox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vox_\(website\))* reported that the news organizations "have to submit all materials and footage to the IDF for review before publication".[^cite_note-Narea-2023-721] The conflict has also seen large numbers of journalists wounded or killed. The [International Federation of Journalists](1178863.md) stated that as many journalists were killed in the first two months of the Gaza war as in the two decades of the [Vietnam War](32611.md).[^cite_note-Price-2023-722] [Reporters Without Borders](526054.md) filed a complaint with the [International Criminal Court](14880.md) under the [Rome Statute](68762.md), accusing Israel of committing war crimes against eight journalists.[^cite_note-rsf20231101-723][^cite_note-Narea-2023-721] It also lodged a complaint against Hamas for the killing of a reporter covering the October 7 attack.[^cite_note-rsf20231101-723] The [Committee to Protect Journalists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Committee_to_Protect_Journalists) accused Israel of targeting journalists reporting from Gaza and their families.[^cite_note-724]&#x20;

["Revealed: repairing Israel's destruction of Gaza will come at huge climate cost"](https://www.theguardian.com/world/article/2024/jun/06/rebuilding-gaza-climate-cost). *[The Guardian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Guardian)*. Retrieved 6 June 2024. The carbon cost of rebuilding Gaza will be greater than the annual greenhouse gas emissions generated individually by 135 countries, exacerbating the global climate emergency on top of the unprecedented death toll, new research reveals. Reconstructing the estimated 200,000 apartment buildings, schools, universities, hospitals, mosques, bakeries, water and sewage plants damaged and destroyed by Israel in the first four months of the war on Gaza will generate as much as 60m tonnes of CO2 equivalent (tCO2e), according to new analysis by researchers in the UK and US. This is on a par with the total 2022 emissions generated by countries such as Portugal and Sweden – and more than twice the annual emissions of Afghanistan.
